## Unavailable episode progress — follow-up in 1.2.2

The supplied 1.2.1 log (copied 2026-09-30 at 23:07:04 +02:00) confirms successful collection, not scan exhaustion: 79 requests finished in about 18.4 seconds with no failure and no pending series. Of 248 series, 247 had usable season plans: 53 were caught up, 14 were unfinished, and 180 remained unknown; one plan was rejected. Of 5,604 episode records checked, 3,673 had an unknown completion result and none had a missing reference. The existing log cannot distinguish absent/error fields, unsupported field shapes, negative bookmarks, or incomplete runtime information. The user identified **Weeds**, **Ozark**, **Chilling Adventures of Sabrina**, and **You** as finished titles still in the main list. Their names match the supplied membership log, but that log contains no per-series episode evidence explaining their classifications.

- **Bounded follow-up lookup:** Finish and publish the ordinary series scan first. Then batch the already-referenced IDs of unknown episodes through the existing read-only `videos` path, using the [client's video/progress fields](https://github.com/CastagnaIT/plugin.video.netflix/blob/master/resources/lib/utils/api_paths.py). Fill missing progress/runtime information from that response, reject explicit non-episode type conflicts, and apply the same watched/90%/credits rule. A series moves only after full exact, unique episode coverage qualifies. This alternate lookup is tested locally; the supplied log does not prove it returns richer fields on this account.
- **Coverage and performance:** Recheck at most 200 unique IDs per request, once per scan. Exclude plans already disproved by unfinished episodes, invalid references, or duplicates. Stop further work for a plan if its direct lookup still supplies unusable or unfinished progress. Resume previously unfetched ranges only after all observed blocking records qualify; do not repeat covered ranges or count a series twice. Ordinary scan results survive a later follow-up failure or budget limit. Both phases share the existing total 96-request/90-second limit and eight-second request ceiling; no extra quota, polling, hover/scroll request, or automatic HTTP retry is added. Unresolved plans retain only temporary normalized records, not response graphs; the persistent grid state retains compact summaries.
- **Parser correction:** Resolve referenced summary, watched, progress, runtime, credits, and count fields through the existing bounded graph reader. The earlier video reader unwrapped atoms but treated leaf references as the values themselves. A local reference fixture reproduces a missed valid completion; missing and cyclic references remain unknown. Reference/atom/error handling follows the [Netflix JSON Graph documentation](https://netflix.github.io/falcor/documentation/jsongraph.html). No numeric-string coercion, negative-bookmark completion assumption, finale-only inference, or series-level watched shortcut is introduced.
- **Diagnostics:** Add aggregate follow-up attempts/recoveries and finite field-kind counters (missing, error, reference, boolean, numeric sign, string, object) to completion logs. Only an explicit **Copy Logs** builds a `seriesViewing` report with current series titles, completion status, checked/expected totals, and reasons such as unavailable progress, unfinished episodes, invalid references, or incomplete season coverage. It includes title-level counts and field-kind summaries, without video/episode IDs, raw payloads, exact progress positions, profile identity, or authentication. Profile changes clear these summaries alongside classifications. The user's four cases are not hardcoded into the userscript; every series receives the same compact report.

Syntax and whitespace checks pass. No assistant browser inspection or authenticated Netflix request was performed. Install/reload 1.2.2, allow checking to finish, and compare the four named series. ## Latest-episode inference and remembered corrections — implemented in 1.3.0

The supplied 1.2.2 log (copied 2026-09-30 at 23:34:40 +02:00) confirms 89 successful requests with no pending series. The ten follow-up requests checked 1,959 unknown episodes and recovered none. Unknown episode records uniformly had a missing watched field, a negative bookmark, and a positive runtime. The named cases were Weeds (99 complete, three unfinished), Sabrina (15 complete, two unfinished, 19 unknown), Ozark (24 complete, one unfinished, 19 unknown), and You (40 complete, one unfinished, nine unknown). The log does not establish the meaning of negative bookmarks or which affected records are finales. Lowering a percentage cannot repair absent usable progress. After discussion, the user explicitly selected both the latest-episode inference with fuller diagnostics and remembered manual corrections.

- **Selected inference:** Keep the movie/episode cutoff at 90%, accepting a valid earlier credits boundary or a positive watched flag. Identify the last episode of the last nonempty season only from a consistent, bounded season plan. Check its range first; its qualifying completion can now move the series despite reset, absent, or unfinished older progress. Missing/conflicting season counts, duplicate observed episode identities, missing finale references, and explicitly wrong video types do not qualify. This deliberately supersedes the earlier requirement that every episode prove completion. It can count a series whose older episodes were skipped; the user accepted that inference for their viewing habits. The endpoint's returned season/episode ordering and coverage still define the latest episode; no separate release/playability contract is established here.
- **Performance and fallback:** Use the same at-most-200-episode requests and shared 96-request/90-second total budget. A 500-episode fixture qualifies after its final 100-record range, avoiding the prior two older-range requests. A missing finale can receive the existing bounded direct lookup even if older episodes look unfinished; in that case recheck only the finale. No new request quota, polling, hover/scroll fetch, or HTTP retry is introduced. Full coverage can still be recorded as verified; partial coverage qualifying from the latest episode is explicitly described as an inference. Retain partial results after later failures and preserve route/profile cancellation.
- **Manual controls and priority:** Add localized **Mark watched** / **Mark caught up**, **Move back to My List**, and a conditional **Use automatic status** action below artwork. Move the existing card immediately, update counts, preserve independent type filters and Netflix membership/native ordering, and send no Netflix request or viewing-history write. An explicit main-list choice also outweighs automatic completion until changed or reset. Build the small action rows within the existing construction chunks, reuse unchanged labels/attributes, and delegate clicks once per grid. Controls cancel obsolete popup preparation, prevent native card navigation, preserve normal artwork hover, survive native clone replacement/rebuild and remove/Undo, and reject obsolete grid/card/profile controls. All five messages cover the existing 31 primary UI locales with English fallback.
- **Memory and new episodes:** Use [Tampermonkey script storage](https://www.tampermonkey.net/documentation.php?q=GM_values), keyed separately by active Netflix profile, with title IDs and a versioned, validated choice map. Add only `GM_getValue` and `GM_setValue` grants. Normal reloads/script updates retain choices in that browser installation; clearing userscript storage or removing the installation can lose them, and cross-device sync is not implemented. Read on profile load and before a write; write only an explicit choice/reset or a changed coverage baseline/expiry. Merge only changed titles into the newest saved map, preserving unrelated choices from another tab; conditional automatic updates preserve newer human choices. This is not an atomic cross-tab transaction or live tab synchronization. Retain at most 5,000 valid stored choices per profile. Storage failures show a localized notice and do not pretend a user choice was saved.
- **Correction expiry:** Remember the validated season identities and per-season lengths when a series is marked caught up. A later consistent plan containing a new nonempty season or additional episodes clears that completion correction and applies the current automatic result. Already automatically complete new episodes can keep the series caught up. Inconsistent/unavailable metadata does not expire it. A choice made before usable metadata arrives adopts the first reliable baseline once; additions before that first baseline cannot be distinguished. A failed expiry write still reveals new episodes in the current grid and reports the persistence failure. Baselines refresh on list initialization or explicit viewing-status refresh, with no background monitor.
- **Diagnostics:** Copy Logs now includes the latest season/episode position, normalized progress percentage (one decimal, capped at 100), threshold, watched flag, credits result, and a reason distinguishing completion, below-threshold progress, unavailable progress, missing reference, or an unchecked finale. Preserve finite raw field-kind summaries for unknown finales. Report effective versus automatic status and a manual-choice flag, without exporting saved profile/season/video IDs, exact playback positions, authentication, or response graphs. Percentages require valid runtime/bookmark values; a negative bookmark remains unavailable rather than guessed complete. These details are generated on explicit Copy Logs, not on scroll/hover.
- **Validation and next check:** All 179 deterministic tests pass, retaining the prior coverage and updating six scenarios whose expected long-series ranges/allocations changed. Twenty-three new regressions cover threshold diagnostics, finale inference and references/types, mixed earlier progress, tail-first multi-season scans, manual moves/counts, reload/refresh/profile isolation, missing metadata/storage failures, new episodes/seasons, baseline capture, stale controls/rebuilds/native replacement, hover cancellation, remove/Undo, unchanged-sync costs, and sequential stale-tab writes. Syntax and whitespace checks pass. No assistant browser inspection or authenticated Netflix request was performed. Install/reload 1.3.0, check the four named series automatically, mark remaining known finished titles, reload to check persistence, and try reverse/reset and normal hover. If recognition still misses a title, Copy Logs can now establish whether a lower cutoff would help its actual finale. History scraping and the remaining unrelated performance/reliability proposals stay unselected.

## Preserve browsing position after viewing corrections — implemented in 1.3.1

The user reported that marking a title watched jumps to the Watched / Caught up section. The supplied 1.3.0 log (copied 2026-10-01 at 00:13:16 +02:00) shows a finished scan with 89 requests, no failure or pending series, and manual corrections reflected in the completion count. It does not record scroll or focus positions. The manual click handler explicitly focused the section summary after the moved card became hidden; [the default focus behavior scrolls the focused element into view](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/HTMLElement/focus), matching the reported jump.

- **Fix:** Keep the focus handoff out of hidden controls, passing `preventScroll: true` to the existing summary focus call. Apply it equally to marking, reversing, and resetting a choice when the destination section or type filter hides the card. Counts, persistence, filters, and the collapsed section continue to work as before. No timers, viewport measurements/writes, polling, or Netflix requests are added.
- **Validation and next check:** Two regressions exercise the actual delegated click handler with a focus mock that reproduces the browser's default scroll. Both fail against 1.3.0 and pass with the fix, covering repeated marks into the collapsed section and reverse/reset into filtered destinations, while retaining focus, storage, grouping, and request checks. All 181 deterministic tests and the syntax/whitespace checks pass. These are local DOM tests, not a live Firefox check; removing cards can still cause ordinary layout changes or end-of-page scroll clamping. Update/reload 1.3.1 and mark titles while browsing the main grid to check the reported forced jump. The remaining performance/reliability proposals stay unselected.

## Earlier viewing groups and smaller series scans — implemented in 1.3.2

The user selected a performance patch for titles appearing in My List and moving into Watched / Caught up over a delayed scan. The code confirmed that movie results stayed private until all title requests, and sometimes the first series group, completed. Every list entry also discarded automatic classifications. Series requests still fetched historical episode ranges and limited grouping by total episode counts, although the selected latest-episode completion rule no longer needs that older progress.

- **Earlier publication:** Publish results, title types, diagnostics, and request counters after each title response, episode response, and direct follow-up batch. Movies no longer wait for later title/series requests. Reuse the existing grouping function and cards, preserving native membership/order, independent filters, manual priority, and hover cancellation. Do not duplicate the old end-of-series-group publication. Request/failure/profile/route ownership and the shared 96-request/90-second ceiling remain intact.
- **Smaller series checks:** Validate the bounded complete season plan as before, then request only the last episode of its last nonempty season. Batch up to 50 series while bounding season metadata and requested finale records to the existing 200-record allowances. A 500-episode series needs one progress record, rather than its last 100-record range; series no longer occupy separate batches merely because their historical totals are large. An unknown finale with a usable reference gets at most one direct lookup after the ordinary scan. Unfinished, missing, conflicting, or unusable finale data stays in the main list. The watched flag, 90% cutoff, and valid earlier credits boundary are unchanged.
- **Completion evidence:** This scan now checks the finale only, using the user's already selected inference. Logs retain full expected episode counts and finale progress, but checked/unknown counts describe the single observed episode; completion is identified as latest-episode inference unless the one-episode plan also establishes complete coverage. Older progress and older duplicate episode references are no longer inspected. Complete and consistent season identities/counts, valid finale references, and episode type checks still qualify the inference; the separate full-coverage classifier remains available for that diagnostic distinction.
- **Warm entry and refresh:** Reuse a compact automatic status/type snapshot for the same active profile before the first viewing response. Validate its schema, completion cutoff, timestamp, at-most-5,000 entries, and current list membership; accept it for at most six hours. Keep it separate from remembered manual corrections, which always win. Revalidate on every entry; replace provisional movie results as their response arrives and provisional series results after their episode check. Same-grid refresh similarly retains current grouping until fresh results arrive. Discard provisional results on verification failure/budget exhaustion, missing context, or profile change, retaining fresh confirmations and explicit manual choices. This can briefly show the previous classification while checking changed viewing progress/new episodes; first use and an expired cache still require Netflix responses.
- **Runtime cost and persistence:** Read the optional cache once per list initialization and write only fresh scan data once on completion, including any confirmed partial results after failure. Never extend unverified entries or save effective manual status as automatic status. Store no response graphs, episode progress positions, authentication, or series history. Cache storage errors fall back to ordinary fresh grouping without disabling working corrections. Add no background polling, timers, request retries, network quota, scroll work, or grid-publication wait. Copy-only series diagnostics flag provisional cached automatic status; start/completion logs include cache reuse/publication counts.
- **Validation and next check:** All 192 deterministic tests pass. Eleven new regressions cover first-batch movie publication, a 500-long-series scan using exactly 500 finale records/30 requests, grouping before the first response, stable browsing storage costs, changed movie/finale progress, manual priority, failed/partial verification, malformed/expired cache data, optional storage errors, profile/route isolation, and same-grid refresh. Update historical-range/diagnostic expectations for the selected finale-only behavior; retain continuation/time/cancellation tests with smaller batch allowances and expand direct-repair coverage to 249 unknown finales in 200/49-record requests. Syntax and whitespace checks pass. No assistant browser inspection or authenticated Netflix request was performed, and request/record reductions do not establish measured browser timing. Other open performance/reliability proposals remain unselected.

## Global performance review — userscript 1.3.2, 2026-10-01

The original review was analysis only, requested by the user. It covered viewing collection/publication/cache/manual choices, grid construction and filtering, hover intent/native page preparation/React grafting/geometry, resize and mutation callbacks, bootstrap/native collection, Undo retention, route recovery, images, and diagnostic/localization work. The user subsequently selected original points 1–4, implemented in 1.3.3 below, then authorized working through the remaining points one by one. Preserve the original numbers and each proposal's measurement gates. Earlier completed and deferred work is excluded from this list, which supersedes the previous open-work table. The table's code locations and evidence describe the 1.3.2 audit; subsequent implementation evidence is recorded below.

Read-only Node probes reused the shipped-function DOM/scheduling harness with test registration disabled; they made no authenticated request and inspected no browser. A 500-movie startup had ten responses and thirteen group synchronizations, revisiting card controls 6,500 times. One warm initial 500-series scan made thirty requests/twenty publications and revisited controls 11,500 times; these visits came from finite startup publications, not ongoing automatic rescanning while browsing. A no-change sync and one manual choice each revisited all 500 cards; marking the last card also cleared an unrelated active first-card hover. One target hover rebuilt all six mounted cards. A resize callback with unchanged dimensions cleared active hover. An Undo tree remained retained after 60 seconds and disappeared only on an explicit prune. Detached readiness blocked session 1; a subsequent run with ready metadata did not call readiness again. These demonstrate control flow and operation counts, not real Netflix frame times, heap size, frequency, or popup compatibility.

| Priority | Proposal | Evidence, benefit, and scope |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 — P1, implemented in 1.3.3 | Establish stable viewing groups and update only affected titles | The existing scan is finite, runs on entry or explicit Refresh, and is not triggered by ordinary scrolling/hovering. The avoidable cost is that `publishViewingProgress` (3362), `syncWatchGroups` (3927), `syncManualViewingCard` (3765), and `reconcileManualViewingCoverage` (3706) revisit the whole list/choice map per initial response or explicit action, even when the cache already supplies the same classification. Group cached/manual results once on entry, apply confirmed first-scan changes only to affected titles, then retain those categories until a meaningful action or list/profile change. Skip unchanged control labels/attributes/order, maintain counts from changes, and combine publications arriving together without losing prompt results. Filters/order changes use stored classification and update visibility/order; initialize or reconcile a rebuilt tree fully when required. Preserve hover if its card and geometry are unaffected; continue cancelling when a target becomes hidden, changes position, or loses its source. Add no periodic reclassification or status watcher. |
| 2 — P1, implemented in 1.3.3 | Recover and clean up native-source replacement correctly | The earlier two lifecycle gaps remain: readiness failure blocks the whole route (`runScript`, 11109), and `resetDetachedTargetState` (1255) discards geometry proxy references without restoring descriptors. Recover a verified replacement with a finite retry, restore alignment before reset, preserve queued deltas, and cancel obsolete owners. Primarily prevents failed initialization/hover and retained stale clone references; live frequency remains unknown. |
| 3 — P2, implemented in 1.3.3 | Refresh/graft only the hovered card when possible | `prepareMountedPage` (9482, replacement loop at 9707) recreates every known mounted-page clone, including unhovered or filtered neighbors. The six-slot probe rebuilt six cards for one target. Limit cloning/React grafting to the requested target and refresh other cards only when actually needed, retaining native identity, wrapped-tail exclusion, source freshness, and cancellation checks. This reduces DOM/fiber allocation and replacement per preparation; Netflix event compatibility needs user-owned validation. |
| 4 — P2, implemented in 1.3.3 | Avoid cancelling hover and remeasuring for irrelevant resize events | `handleTargetWindowResize` (10684) and `handleTargetVisualViewportResize` (10698) cancel first; `scheduleResponsiveRefresh` (10558) discovers unchanged geometry later. Gate work using relevant width/scale/position/page-shape changes, with a lightweight coalesced geometry check and conservative handling of genuinely changed bounds. Reuse measurements within one check and avoid reapplying unchanged styles. A height-only event is a candidate, not permission to ignore height or viewport movement universally. Reduces redundant layout work and avoidable hover loss. |
| 5 — P2, implemented in 1.3.4; supplied live check supports retaining cap two | Overlap a small number of independent viewing requests | `collectViewingStatuses` (3373) awaited all title batches, then each independent series batch in order. The bounded implementation overlaps independent batches while retaining dependencies, quotas, cancellation and partial results. The paired 1.3.8 logs below show actual overlap and successful completion without throttling; retain cap two. One successful visit does not establish a controlled speed-up or universal endpoint compatibility. |
| 6 — P2, implemented in 1.3.5 | Release expired Undo trees promptly | `pruneUndoEntries` (4881) is called only on later removal/Undo events. Use one route-owned next-expiry schedule to release a card after the 30-second retention window while preserving a queued mutation's own fallback. Confirmed retained-reference improvement; likely a modest memory benefit unless many cards are removed. |
| 7 — P2, implemented in 1.3.6 | Reuse an already verified complete single-page list | `tryMountedSinglePageFastBootstrap` (6616) qualifies complete normal-SPA membership, but `collectLogicalItems` (4665) still fetches because its bootstrap has no GraphQL edges. Collect the verified native cards directly for that qualified case, retaining count/index/identity stability and fresh bootstrap for manual/order-mismatch entry. Saves one redundant membership request for short lists; offers little benefit to a 500-title list. |
| 8 — P3, implemented in 1.3.7 | Deduplicate native card metadata before capturing snapshots | `collectAllItemsLogical` (8008) and `collectAllItems` (8425) still call snapshot-capturing `itemFromSlot` (6946) before duplicate checks. Validate identity/index/order, then clone only accepted cards in the same synchronous sample. Reduces allocation for overlapping/repeated native windows, mainly on fallback scans; retain detection of titles moving during collection. |
| 9 — P3, measurements assessed; diagnostic gap addressed in 1.3.9 | Tighten thumbnail request timing and reserve verified image geometry | `createItemClone` (6975) assigns GraphQL artwork before `normalizeClone` (8644) applies lazy/async image settings. The paired 1.3.8 snapshots show existing lazy/async hints, complete current images and sampled boxes, but saved resource history cannot account for the visit. Bounded future resource observations in 1.3.9 address that gap. No request/decode/layout evidence yet justifies changing loading or sizing policy; the user's stutter is now specifically associated with an open/opening hover popup. |
| 10 — P3, measure first | Render fewer offscreen rows if layout/paint remains a bottleneck | `buildGrid` (10049) constructs/publishes all cards; filtering and collapsed details hide cards but retain their trees. If browser traces show costly visible-grid layout/paint or DOM retention after smaller fixes, assess stable row windowing/offscreen rendering. Preserve hover overflow/geometry, keyboard focus, manual actions, counts, ordering, browser find/accessibility, and scroll anchors. This has the largest structural risk; chunking already addresses construction stalls, so do not assume virtualization is required. |

An adjacent low-risk optimization is resolving shared button text and reusing `Intl.NumberFormat`/`Intl.PluralRules` instances per locale/options (`formatUiNumber`, 985; `tUiPlural`, 964). It remains unselected: 1.3.3 skips unchanged controls/count displays, substantially reducing repeated translation/formatting calls, and formatter construction's remaining runtime share has not been measured. No blanket observer rewrite, extra polling, threshold change, or longer cache lifetime is proposed. Original points 1–4 are complete; evaluate remaining network and allocation work independently. Image/rendering proposals still require browser evidence supplied by the user.

## Discussion refinement — stable categories and performance patterns

The user clarified that viewing categories should be stable during a list visit after initial loading, with manual corrections as the ordinary reason to move a title. The initial scan already stops; no repeated background viewing refresh needs to be removed. Rechecking the call sites confirms that entry and the explicit Refresh control start viewing collection. Batch publication, manual controls, filtering, membership/order reconciliation, and grid rebuilds can call group synchronization; ordinary scroll/hover does not initiate viewing collection. Thus priority 1 addresses redundant whole-grid bookkeeping during the finite initial scan and explicit changes, rather than recurring status drift. Cached/manual results still appear immediately, and newly confirmed results still update promptly while the first scan completes. Actual profile/list changes, explicit refresh, and required rebuilds retain their consistency checks.

The ten proposals group into these patterns, ordered by the highest-priority finding in each group. Original proposal numbers remain stable for reference:

1. **Avoid repeating work when inputs are unchanged — 1 and 4.** Establish categories once, apply only affected-title updates, and keep unchanged card controls/hover intact. Similarly, irrelevant resize events should not cancel hover or repeat geometry/style work. Category updates and resize handling share the principle but can be separate patches.
2. **Recover and release owned resources — 2 and 6.** Restore native-source geometry overrides before dropping references, permit bounded recovery from replacement, and release expired Undo card trees. Native recovery is the higher-priority reliability fix; Undo expiry is a smaller independent memory fix.
3. **Prepare only the cards that need expensive work — 3 and 8.** Clone/graft the actual hover target, and reject duplicate native metadata before taking card snapshots. These reduce DOM/React allocation at different points; hover compatibility and native collection should be validated separately.
4. **Shorten initial loading and avoid redundant requests — 5 and 7.** Reuse a verified complete single-page list and assess limited overlap of independent viewing requests. Short-list reuse is a concrete redundancy; concurrency remains an experiment within current budgets and cancellation rules.
5. **Change rendering only when measurements justify it — 9 and 10.** Investigate thumbnail request/decode/layout behavior before changing image policy, then consider offscreen rows only if significant layout/paint costs remain. These conditional proposals are separate from the confirmed operation-count reductions.

This discussion grouped the proposals conceptually without authorizing implementation. Keep native recovery, hover preparation, resize behavior, and request concurrency independently reviewable because they have different failure modes and validation requirements. No userscript/test change or new timing measurement was made for that refinement. The user's later selection of original points 1–4 is recorded below.

## Targeted groups, native recovery, hover and resize — implemented in 1.3.3

The user selected original review points 1–4 and requested diagnostic evidence for this and future performance/reliability patches. Recorded the durable logging preference in `context.md`; the other proposals remain unselected.

- **1 — Stable viewing groups:** Initialize one ordered group index for the displayed tree. Each title/finale/direct response supplies only its affected IDs; confirmed results replace provisional cache entries as they arrive. Reuse unchanged entries without reading card attributes, rebuilding controls, walking group children, or rewriting counts/status. Maintain group/type/unknown counts from changes, and insert moved cards in native-relative order. Type filters use established classifications and touch only the relevant group's visibility; storage/profile/locale changes still update affected controls. Manual saves reconcile changed choices from the latest stored map so another tab's unrelated corrections are retained. Coverage reconciliation considers the relevant titles, including manual baseline capture/expiry. Full reconciliation remains for real membership/order changes, profile changes, rebuilt trees, and failure fallbacks. Hover clone replacement updates the index's card reference. Protected active/pending hover survives when visibility/geometry/source remain valid and cancels when its card moves, hides, detaches or loses its source. The scan remains finite with no scroll-triggered viewing work or periodic refresh. Sequential requests still publish promptly per response; redundant DOM work is skipped instead of adding a publication-delay timer.
- **2 — Native-source lifecycle:** Restore geometry descriptors and temporary carousel styles before detached reset loses references. Native-source/readiness failure may retry once per route visit after a connected replacement section/scroller/track is verified; an unchanged source does not qualify. A later native mutation can unblock a failed visit without another full page load. Rebind the observer, use the existing fresh SPA count/bootstrap path, stop obsolete hover/resize owners, and retain/defer queued list deltas until complete publication. An exhausted retry remains an explicit initialization error. Responsive settling/refresh checks its captured state/source/grid after awaits, and old completion cannot clear a newer refresh's flags. Normal route cleanup resets failure ownership/budget.
- **3 — Target-only hover preparation:** Preserve native identity/position, wrapped-tail exclusion, cancellation and replay checks, but clone/graft/replace only the requested card. Unhovered neighbors prepare on their own later hover; a genuinely changed native page assignment still updates lightweight metadata. Prior native grafts are invalidated when their mounted source page becomes obsolete.
- **4 — Resize work:** Duplicate window/visual-viewport events preserve hover and coalesce into one scoped geometry sample. Reuse section/slot rectangles within that synchronous sample. Skip unchanged styles, status formatting and full refresh when both native layout and applied grid geometry remain unchanged. Actual window/visual bounds, zoom, pixel-ratio or offset changes still cancel hover promptly; height is not universally ignored. Changed gaps/heights, viewport clipping, page shape or stale native mapping still take the guarded refresh path. Preserve the existing page-count-only convergence shortcut and discard checks belonging to an old state/route. No automatic hover replay follows resize.
- **Diagnostic evidence:** Copy Logs runtime snapshots include `performanceWork` with aggregate viewing-group, hover-preparation, resize and recovery counters. Viewing completion includes a copied counter snapshot; manual-choice logs report work for that action, success and changed-title count. Existing native preparation logs add `preparationScope`, `slotsConsidered`, `neighborsSkipped` and rebuilt-card count. Recovery logs identify the reason, one-attempt budget, queued delta count and exhaustion. Failed geometry restoration records a bounded warning/counter. Unchanged resize events increment counters without constructing a verbose event log. Counters reset per route visit, contain no added title/profile/authentication/DOM data, and add no polling, per-card timers or persistence writes.
- **Validation:** All 213 deterministic tests pass, including 21 new regressions for operation reductions, unchanged card/layout reads, targeted manual changes, preserved/cancelled hover, stored-status filtering, clone ownership, immediate/late bounded recovery and queued deltas, descriptor restoration/failure diagnostics, target-only and wrapped-tail preparation, resize coalescing/bounds/clipping/geometry, scoped rectangle reuse, diagnostic snapshot isolation and stale responsive owners. A warm 500-series scan still makes 30 requests/20 publications, but card-control visits fall from 11,500 to 500 and all full initial grouping occurs once; its 1,500 lightweight title checks are the initial entries plus finite title/finale batches. One manual move visits one control instead of 500. A six-slot hover rebuilds one card instead of six. Forty duplicate resize callbacks produce one measurement, zero style updates/full refreshes, and retained hover in the fixture. These are code-path/count results, not measured Netflix frame times or heap sizes. Syntax, version consistency and whitespace checks pass; no assistant browser inspection or authenticated Netflix request was performed.

## Limited viewing-request overlap — experiment implemented in 1.3.4

The user authorized working through the remaining open points one by one. At release 1.3.4, this patch addressed original point **5** as a controlled two-request experiment, with adoption awaiting real Netflix latency/throttling evidence. The paired 1.3.8 evaluation below now supports retaining cap two. At that release, point 6 was the next independent implementation step. Points 9–10 still require evidence before a rendering-policy change; the adjacent formatter-cache idea remains outside the selected ten points.

- **Scheduling:** Run independent title batches and independent series batches in finite waves of at most two. Each series chain still waits for season metadata before requesting its finale. Restore native title order before forming series batches, regardless of response arrival order. Title collection finishes before series collection; direct repairs and My List cursor pagination remain sequential. The optional cross-phase pipeline is omitted to keep ownership and budget priority straightforward. Each response still publishes only its affected titles promptly, including when its peer is pending; no publication-delay timer or whole-grid regrouping is added.
- **Reliability:** Reserve requests synchronously against the same 32-request passes/three-pass limit and combined 90-second deadline, with the existing eight-second individual timeout. Near the total quota, reduce series overlap to leave room for a chain's finale. Stop scheduling after a known error, without HTTP retries. Drain already allocated same-owner reads before finalizing and saving confirmed partial results. Route/profile cancellation aborts the scan's own pending controllers; a profile cancellation leaves unrelated route requests owned. Await cleanup before completion so a late peer cannot update a finalized scan, and retain cache/manual-choice priority and one completion write.
- **Diagnostics:** Start logs report `concurrencyLimit`. Completion, failure/cancellation and Copy Logs runtime snapshots expose copied scalar `network` metrics: started/succeeded/failed requests, current/peak concurrency, elapsed collection time, cumulative/mean/maximum request duration, actual time with overlapping requests, HTTP 429 count and aborted-read count. Completion timing stops when collection drains, rather than increasing during later browsing. No response graphs, titles, profile/authentication values, per-request verbose events or persistent telemetry are added.
- **Validation:** All 220 deterministic tests pass, including seven new scheduling/ownership regressions. Deferred responses verify two-request bounds, out-of-order prompt publication and native ordering, dependency ordering, shared caps, valid partial completion after HTTP 429, no new finale after a known metadata error, both-request route/profile cancellation, unrelated controller isolation, deadline/individual timeouts, no retries and no timer/controller leaks. Older ordinal-request fixtures now target the relevant title/finale, and the slow-response fixture advances shared wall time once for parallel reads. A modeled pair of 100 ms title reads completes in 100 ms with overlap versus 200 ms sequentially, making the same two requests; seven modeled seven-second series reads use 35 seconds of wall time and 49 seconds of summed request duration. The 500-series regression still makes 30 requests/20 publications with 500 control visits. These demonstrate scheduling and operation counts, not measured Netflix speed or endpoint compatibility. Syntax, matching release versions and whitespace are checked; no assistant browser inspection or authenticated Netflix request is performed.

The requested live evaluation compared `network.elapsedMs`, request durations, `overlapMs`, `peakInFlight` and HTTP failures/429s after a completed scan. The supplied paired 1.3.8 logs and their interpretation are recorded below; there is no matched sequential control visit from which to claim a precise speed-up.

## Prompt Undo snapshot expiry — implemented in 1.3.5

This separate patch completes original point **6**. The Undo cache now has one route-owned next-expiry timer. A removal retains its existing card for 30 seconds; expiry removes the cache entry without requiring a subsequent click or explicit prune. Staggered removals retain the current earliest timer, and consuming/replacing the earliest entry reschedules to the next deadline. Consuming the final entry cancels the timer. Expiry is inclusive at 30 seconds, avoiding a zero-delay loop at the boundary. The timer captures scalar owner data only; source reset and route suspension clear it along with the cache, and an obsolete callback cannot clear a newer timer.

Expiry removes the Undo cache's reference rather than clearing the item's snapshot. A pending add/Undo mutation may independently own that same fallback item and can still complete after cache expiry; normal mutation disposal and successful grid publication release that ownership. This does not add a per-card timer, polling, an HTTP request, or a change to the visible grid.

Copy Logs runtime snapshots expose the current retained-entry count, timer presence and next expiry delay. `performanceWork.undoRetention` counts remembered, expired, consumed and cleared entries, timer schedules and expiry callbacks. An actual expiry emits one aggregate event with expired/remaining counts and the number of preserved pending fallbacks; no card/title/profile information is added. No expiry event is emitted for an empty cache.

All 224 deterministic tests pass. Four new regressions verify idle release immediately at the 30-second boundary, one earliest timer across staggered removals, rescheduling/cancellation after Undo, mutation-owned fallback preservation and eventual successful application, route cleanup and stale-callback isolation. The existing queued-removal/grid-publication regression now also checks its future Undo timer and automatic release; construction draining advances only currently due task yields, leaving the future retention timer owned. Existing snapshot/React ownership, profile/manual grouping, route/native recovery and bounded-request checks remain passing. Syntax, matching release versions and whitespace checks pass. This verifies retained references and lifecycle behavior, not measured browser heap savings; no assistant browser inspection or authenticated Netflix request was performed.

The full follow-up plan remains active.

## Verified single-page membership reuse — implemented in 1.3.6

This separate patch completes original point **7**. A normal SPA route entry that passes the existing two frame-separated, complete single-page qualification carries a transient source/signature/route proof into logical collection. After readiness, collection verifies the current section/scroller/track ownership, active route and normal entry reason, mounted React count agreement, complete ordered indices, unique identities, count/column bounds and unchanged signature. It reads card metadata without cloning, validates every title identity, then captures the accepted native card variants in the same synchronous sample. Reuse goes directly into the normal grid publication path, without a membership request or native navigation; temporary native snapshots are released after publication.

Initial entry, manual/order-mismatch reinitialization, partial or larger lists, conflicting count readings, duplicate/shifted identities, source replacement, changed layout/membership, missing proof and incomplete card metadata retain fresh collection. Route cancellation still propagates without requests or stale publication. A rejected reuse uses the existing GraphQL collection/fallback behavior and does not introduce HTTP retries. The proof retains only the initialization's source references/signature and is excluded from logs, storage and runtime snapshots.

`performanceWork.membershipReuse` reports attempts, successful/rejected reuse, captured-item count and avoided membership requests. Prepared/used collection logs identify `collectionSource`, successful preparation reports the avoided request, and rejected reuse reports a compact reason. Existing GraphQL collection logs remain identifiable. No new polling, per-card timer, quota, title-history storage or whole-grid classification work is added.

All 230 deterministic tests pass, including six new qualification/collection regressions. A complete six-card mounted SPA list uses zero membership requests and zero native scans, preserves each card's markup/artwork/variant, captures exactly six native snapshots and releases them after grid publication. Partial/duplicate/shifted/conflicting/unstable/disconnected/indicator samples cannot qualify. Changes after qualification to membership, index, count, source, layout or metadata reject reuse before cloning and perform the existing fresh request; initial/manual/order-mismatch entry also keeps its fresh request. An obsolete route cannot collect or publish the proof. Existing cursor ordering, count mismatch, pagination failure/timeout, native recovery, snapshot ownership, viewing budgets and Undo expiry checks remain passing. Syntax, matching release versions and whitespace checks pass. Request/operation counts are verified locally; no browser latency benefit is claimed, and no assistant browser inspection or authenticated Netflix request was performed.

## Native metadata deduplication before snapshots — implemented in 1.3.7

This separate patch completes original point **8**. Both logical and indicator native fallback collectors read `itemFromSlot` metadata without a snapshot first. The logical collector retains invalid-index, changed-content-at-index and title-moved-to-another-index checks before deciding whether the item is an already known duplicate; it does not skip those checks simply because a title was seen. The indicator collector retains its existing stable-window/new-item, key and ordered-membership rules. Only an accepted item clones its source slot, within the same synchronous sample before navigation or another await. Duplicate and missing-card metadata allocate no snapshot, while the first accepted native variant, canonical logical index/page and native relative order are preserved.

`performanceWork.nativeCollection` counts metadata reads, captured snapshots, duplicate snapshots avoided, incomplete metadata and logical consistency failures. Existing page-result/completion logs include a copied `snapshotWork` counter snapshot; logical incomplete-collection details also include it. These are cumulative counters for the route visit, not per-card verbose events. No source card, DOM tree or additional title/profile data is retained by the counters, and no timer, polling, request, collection budget or navigation rule changes.

All 234 deterministic tests pass. Four new regressions exercise both shipped collector loops through modeled native windows. Two overlapping three-card windows containing four unique titles capture four trees instead of six in both modes, retain each first accepted variant and order, and preserve logical index coverage/page assignment. Changed logical-index content, a title moving to an unoccupied index and an invalid logical index still fail, now before cloning the conflicting card. Incomplete metadata is skipped without a tree and can be covered by a later valid window. Route cancellation takes no snapshots from an obsolete next window and still restores temporary styles and unregisters cleanup ownership. Existing native index/readiness, GraphQL collection/pagination, grid publication, hover, manual grouping, request budgets and Undo tests remain passing. Syntax, matching release versions and whitespace checks pass. This verifies allocation counts and collector behavior, not measured Netflix frame time or heap savings; no assistant browser inspection or authenticated Netflix request was performed.

## Thumbnail measurement gate — diagnostics added in 1.3.8

The user reports that scrolling still stutters after updating to 1.3.7 and letting the initial viewing scan finish. This release addresses original point **9** with read-only measurements, without changing loading hints, artwork dimensions, CSS sizing or grid rendering. Point 9 remains open until live evidence supports a policy decision; this release is not a claimed stutter fix. Point 10 remains conditional on evidence of costly layout/paint or retained DOM.

Reassessment of the assignment-order suspicion: `createItemClone` assigns artwork and `normalizeClone` sets lazy/async in the same synchronous construction callback. The [HTML image-update algorithm](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/images.html#updating-the-image-data) defers relevant processing to a microtask and coalesces successive attribute changes; already available images also have a cache path. The source order alone therefore does not establish an unnecessary request. It remains appropriate to verify runtime behavior rather than change loading policy on that inference. The async decoding setting is a browser hint, not a measurement of image decoding cost.

`thumbnailDiagnostics` is appended only when constructing a Copy Logs export. Routine runtime snapshots, scroll/hover handlers and viewing publications do not invoke it. It inventories the first image in up to 600 mapped, connected grid-owned cards, counts lazy/async hints, pending/complete/pixel states and paired width/height attributes, and separates render-eligible cards from type-filtered and collapsed watched cards. It reports its limits, truncation, skipped stale/missing trees and total elapsed collection time. Responsive `srcset` images with no selected URL yet remain sourced/pending rather than being mislabeled source-less; malformed URLs do not discard otherwise useful card measurements.

A fixed sample of at most 24 render-eligible images is spread across the eligible inventory. It reads at most 48 image/immediate-parent rectangles and 48 computed styles, using visual-viewport offsets where available. Aggregates describe position **at copy time**, pending images with/without a box, immediate-parent boxes, dimension ranges, aspect-ratio hints and parent block padding. A parent box can include controls or unrelated content; neither it nor a dimension hint proves reserved artwork space. Filtered/collapsed cards are counted without measuring their layout. Unsupported style APIs and failed geometry reads are explicitly reported without breaking the export.

The resource probe reads the existing browser Resource Timing buffer and inspects at most its latest 2,000 entries, matching only inventoried HTTP(S) artwork sources with image initiators. It excludes matching requests started before a known list initialization and reports matched/absent sources, buffer truncation, fetch-duration aggregates, explicitly reported cache delivery and available/zero/unreported transfer sizes. It exports no URLs, resource objects, card IDs, DOM references or new title/profile/authentication data, and retains no measurement state between copies. It adds no observer, timer, HTTP request, induced image decode, buffer-policy change or normal-browsing DOM work.

Interpretation limits are explicit: browser resource history can be incomplete; a currently offscreen loaded image could have been viewed previously or loaded under the browser's lazy-load policy. This probe does not know position at request time and does not measure image decoding, paint or layout shifts. Zero transfer size does not establish a cache hit because cross-origin timing fields can be restricted; fetch duration is not decode/paint duration. These constraints follow the [Resource Timing specification](https://www.w3.org/TR/resource-timing/). No decision to reserve a fixed artwork shape, alter loading policy or virtualize rows has been made.

All 239 deterministic tests pass. Five new regressions and an updated Copy Logs integration check verify pending/hidden/complete states, bounded inventory/geometry/resource inspection, anonymous scalar exports, API and geometry failures, visual-viewport offsets, native sizing hints, malformed/unselected URLs, obsolete/disconnected/foreign ownership, no induced decode or DOM attribute changes and no new timer/frame ownership. Existing viewing/grouping/hover/collection/Undo/request-budget checks remain passing. Syntax, matching increased release versions and whitespace checks pass. These fixtures validate the probe, not browser frame time, network/decode behavior, actual layout shifts or the cause of the user's stutter. No assistant browser inspection or authenticated Netflix request was performed.

The requested paired assessment used 1.3.8: let the initial viewing scan finish at the top, save Copy Logs before browsing, then reproduce the scrolling stutter and save another export in the same visit. The user supplied both snapshots; results and the remaining evidence gaps are recorded below.
