The following assessment supersedes those validation statuses.

## Paired scrolling evidence and bounded image observations — 1.3.9

The user supplied before/after 1.3.8 logs copied at 12:41:06 and 12:46:12 on 2026-10-01. Both describe the same route session with 500 mapped grid cards in Firefox 157.0, viewport 2560 × 1279 and device-pixel ratio one. The user then clarified that the stutter occurs when a hover popup opens and when scrolling with it open; scrolling with the pointer away from cards is not the reported trigger. No assistant browser inspection or authenticated Netflix request was performed.

- **Stable categories and completed network work:** Viewing group counters are identical: 22 synchronizations, one full synchronization, 1,425 considered cards, 500 control updates and 196 category moves. The viewing scan remains finished at 23 successful requests, zero failures/429s/aborts and zero in flight. It took 1,714 ms, with peak concurrency two, 2,954 ms summed request duration and 1,251 ms actual overlap. This successful live sample supports retaining original point **5** at cap two; it does not establish the speed-up versus sequential reads or guarantee future endpoint behavior. No repeated scan or regrouping occurred while browsing.
- **Hover is the strongest current suspect:** There are 12 new preparations, 97 completed native-carousel moves, four completed preparations and eight cancelled preparations. Only four cards are rebuilt, with 28 neighboring slots skipped, so the earlier per-target clone reduction remains effective. Individual moves took up to 3,333 ms and 1,535 ms; the former obsolete hover held the serialized queue while later hover attempts waited or cancelled. These are elapsed waits, not measured synchronous blocking or browser frame time. Native recovery stays zero; eight alignment restorations have zero failures. Resize checks increase by one unchanged check, with no additional full refresh or hover cancellation during browsing.
- **Thumbnail evidence is limited:** Both snapshots report 500 lazy/async first images, complete current requests with intrinsic dimensions and paired width/height attributes; all 24 sampled eligible images have boxes and image/parent aspect-ratio hints. This is not proof that intended replacement artwork decoded or painted, nor a layout-shift recording. Current distinct image sources increase from 95 to 278, while saved timing history remains at 250 entries/139 image entries with one matching post-initialization image request. `truncated: false` means the script's 2,000-entry inspection cap was not reached; it does not establish complete browser history. No conclusion about absent new requests or an artwork-source bug follows. Opening Watched also increases eligible cards from 135 to 251, so the snapshots are not an identical-population paint comparison.
- **Smaller startup observation:** Native scroller height changes from 173.6 to one when parked. The initial ResizeObserver refresh cancelled an early hover; no repeated equivalent refresh appears afterward. Retain this as a startup reliability observation, separate from the reported popup/scroll stutter.

Original point **9** now has a bounded diagnostic follow-up. `performanceWork.imageResources` aggregates future Resource Timing entries through a route-owned PerformanceObserver, independently of the saved history queried by Copy Logs. The [Performance Timeline specification](https://www.w3.org/TR/performance-timeline/) queues observer entries before deciding whether the global saved-entry buffer can retain them. The observer examines at most 4,000 resource entries per list route, counts only `img` initiators in its image totals, and disconnects on the cap or route suspension. The budget counts non-image entries too, and reaching it explicitly stops further observation; it is not a promise to capture unlimited browsing. Unsupported/failed APIs report fixed reasons without interrupting the list. Owner checks ignore stale callbacks, and a new route resets its scalar counters.

These aggregates include native Netflix images and hover artwork as well as grid thumbnails. They report image fetch counts/durations, entries started after a known finished viewing scan, the last image-start offset, explicitly reported cache delivery and available transfer bytes. They never read or retain resource URLs, response data or DOM trees, resize/clear the global timing buffer, poll, induce decode, or add network requests. Zero transfer size remains insufficient to infer cache delivery. Copy Logs additionally counts whether a GraphQL-assigned image's selected URL matches its current `src`, differs, or is unresolved; it exports neither URL. Differences are an observation, not proof of the cause or an instruction to discard Netflix picture variants. Routine scrolling performs no new geometry reads through these diagnostics. No loading, sizing, hover or rendering policy changes in this release.

All 243 deterministic tests pass. Four new regressions verify observation beyond 250 saved entries without URL/global-buffer access, the 4,000-entry cap and stale-owner isolation, unsupported/throwing APIs and route cleanup, and anonymous selected-versus-assigned source counts. Existing Copy Logs scalar snapshots remain independently copied. Syntax, matching increased release versions and whitespace checks pass. The fixtures establish bounds, exported data and lifecycle behavior; browser animation, decode, paint and actual frame-time cost remain unmeasured.

## Popup opening and scroll release — follow-up selected after 1.3.9

The user's clarification makes the popup path a higher-priority investigation than generic offscreen rendering. The script prepares a live Netflix card by moving its hidden native carousel, grafts React data onto the requested grid clone, temporarily proxies native geometry to the clone, then replays native hover. Each native move can force layout through style suppression/restoration and polls page identity/geometry. In 1.3.9, on the first scroll event of a burst, `handleTargetScroll` cancelled preparation and restored that geometry, cleared active identifiers and grafts, without explicitly sending the native source a matching hover exit. Whether Netflix's own delegated events closed the popup in time was not established by those logs. A popup still animating or querying geometry after the bridge is removed is a concrete lifecycle question, not a proven cause.

The recommended assessment/patch scope was to trace popup activation, native replay and scroll/leave cleanup; establish one orderly release of an active popup before restoring its geometry if the native event contract supports that; stop obsolete preparation and avoid redundant replay/alignment. Add inexpensive scalar counters and phase durations to distinguish queue delay, page movement, graft/alignment/replay and scroll release, and regressions for rapid scroll/leave/rehover and wrong-title/late-write prevention. Keep the current scroll-intent guard and serialization of already-clicked moves: a Netflix click cannot be undone, and releasing the queue early could let a late native write corrupt the next hover. Do not merely lower all timeouts or suppress Netflix previews on the assumption that this will fix animation. The user subsequently selected this focused follow-up; its implementation is recorded below, separately from original point 10.

At release 1.3.9, original-plan status was **1–8 implemented**, with **5** supported by the supplied successful bounded-overlap visit; **9** had assessed live snapshots and the 1.3.9 diagnostic follow-up, but loading/sizing changes remained conditional; **10** remained an assessment only if layout/paint/DOM evidence justified it. Neither these snapshots nor the user's narrowed trigger established a need for forced image ratios or virtualization. The adjacent formatter optimization remained outside the selected ten points. A Copy Logs export after reproducing popup stutter would supplement the existing pair with resource observations; the newer follow-up below also adds hover measurements.

## Popup cleanup and less hover preparation — implemented in 1.3.10

The user authorized the focused hover follow-up. Source inspection confirmed two avoidable operations in addition to the cleanup gap: both fresh and ready-source activations aligned the native source before a replay frame that immediately cleared and rebuilt the same proxy; `goToPage` also attempted cyclic shortest-path navigation on Hawkins logical carousels, despite the existing non-wrapping boundary contract. The supplied logs include an unacknowledged left move at page zero and a boundary fallback; these waits can delay later hovers without proving continuous main-thread blocking.

- **Owned native exit:** A successful native replay retains one transient source/card identity and its coordinates. Before `clearSourceAlignment` restores source geometry, it sends bubbling `pointerout`/`mouseout` to that same connected card. Ownership is cleared before dispatch, so repeated wheel/scroll cleanup and nested release cannot send another exit. Ordinary delegated pointer leave forwards its actual destination, including a Netflix popup control, rather than inventing a leave to empty space. [React's enter/leave event plugin](https://github.com/react/react/blob/main/packages/react-dom-bindings/src/events/plugins/EnterLeaveEventPlugin.js) uses out events and their related destination for this contract; this does not identify Netflix's exact deployed React version or establish its popup behavior. Source replacement, route teardown, actual resize and other existing alignment-release paths also dispose of the owned replay. Changed/detached/recycled cards receive no stale exit; checks between event families stop remaining events when native handlers change the source. Dispatch failures cannot prevent geometry restoration, and an exit is not automatically retried. No popup DOM selector or visibility polling is added.
- **Single alignment:** Fresh and ready-source paths defer geometry proxy installation until the replay frame's existing identity/active-source revalidation. Successful activations now align once instead of twice. Target-only cloning/grafting and the bounded one-retry recovery remain; source/identity loss still fails safely. Native entry replay checks ownership, cancellation, route and card identity between events, preventing the remaining entries from reopening an obsolete popup after scrolling starts during dispatch.
- **No known boundary detour:** Hawkins logical navigation uses the direct direction instead of attempting a wrapping shortcut. Other/unknown carousel profiles retain their cyclic behavior and direct fallback. The change avoids the ineffective edge click and backtracking along a non-wrapping route; distant cards still require native page traversal. Already-clicked movement retains its serialized acknowledgement and settlement, unchanged timeouts, style restoration and cancellation cadence. No asynchronous queue slot is released early.
- **Verification logs:** Copy Logs exposes scalar `hoverLifecycle` counts for replay/cancellation/failure, native exits/skips/failures, scroll bursts/exits, avoided boundary detours and removed preliminary alignments. The detour counter increases only when cyclic selection would choose the opposite direction from the direct route. `hoverTiming` reports sample counts, summed durations and maxima for queue wait, native movement, clone/graft/normalization, source validation/alignment, event replay and exit dispatch. Captured timing owners cannot write into a newer route's counters. Native move durations include asynchronous acknowledgement/settlement and are not synchronous blocking measurements; graft timing includes clone construction and normalization, and alignment includes source validation. Exit/replay counters establish event dispatch, not actual popup visibility or smooth animation. `nativeHoverOwned` reports only a boolean in the runtime snapshot. No new polling, observer, HTTP request, per-card timer, retained diagnostic DOM/resource object or title/profile/authentication field is added.

All 254 deterministic tests pass. Eleven new regressions cover one alignment for fresh/ready cards, exit before geometry restoration, repeated/nested release, post-scroll stationary suppression, real pointer destinations, detached/recycled/changed native sources, source changes during entry/exit, dispatch failures, cancellation during native entry, rapid rehover/stale replay isolation, route timing ownership, and direct Hawkins versus retained legacy cyclic navigation. The existing clicked-move serialization regression also checks queue/movement timings; previous retry fixtures now drive the frame where alignment actually runs. Syntax, matching increased release versions and whitespace checks pass. These verify shipped control flow, operation counts and cleanup; native popup controls, animation and actual browser frame-time improvement remain user-owned validation. No assistant browser inspection or authenticated Netflix request was performed.

Current ordered follow-up: **first validate 1.3.10's popup opening, scrolling with an open popup, and movement into its controls**, then use one Copy Logs export after reproducing any remaining stutter to compare `hoverLifecycle`, `hoverTiming`, existing native move logs and `imageResources`. Original **9** remains conditional on request/decode/layout evidence; original **10** remains conditional on layout/paint/DOM evidence. Original **1–8** are implemented, and the adjacent formatter idea remains unselected. No image-sizing/loading policy, virtualization, preview suppression or broader React-bridge redesign is included in this patch.

## Remaining hover delay — paired 1.3.10 evidence and 1.3.11 diagnostics

The user supplied before/after logs copied at 14:21:14 and 14:21:53 on 2026-10-01 and reports that popup opening still stutters, sometimes requires repeated hovers, and sometimes takes two to three seconds. Both exports describe route session four with 500 grid cards, 63 native pages and eight columns in Firefox 157. The live result does **not** confirm that 1.3.10 solved the reported responsiveness/animation issue. Its operation reductions and owned cleanup remain covered by deterministic tests. The user explicitly authorizes better diagnostics when existing logs leave uncertainty; that durable preference is now recorded in `context.md`.

- **Background work stays stable:** Both exports retain 22 viewing-group synchronizations, one full synchronization, 1,425 considered cards, 500 control updates and 196 category moves. The viewing scan remains finished with 23 successful requests, zero failures/429s/aborts and zero in flight; elapsed collection is 1,664 ms, peak concurrency two and actual overlap 1,241 ms. Resize adds one unchanged check and no additional refresh/cancellation. No repeated viewing scan or whole-grid regrouping occurred during browsing.
- **Preparation still contributes latency:** Twenty native preparations cause 143 carousel moves, 13 rebuilt targets and 87 skipped neighbors. Aggregate queue wait is zero; moves total 7,467 ms with a 390 ms maximum. Several successful preparations take 0.8–1.4 seconds before native replay. These are elapsed asynchronous preparation/movement waits, not evidence of 7.5 seconds of continuous JavaScript blocking. Graft/normalization totals nine milliseconds, alignment four, replay four and exit dispatch two; these paths are much cheaper in this sample. There are no recorded move timeouts or boundary detours. The visits were not controlled identical-pointer runs, so fewer timeouts do not establish a measured overall speed-up.
- **A repeatable release needs explanation:** Ten successful replays are followed by pointer-leave cleanup roughly 416–426 ms later; the remaining owned exit is from scrolling. This timing may coincide with Netflix opening its preview and changing hit testing. The previous log cannot distinguish that from deliberate pointer movement into another target or a preview control, and it does not record actual popup visibility. Do not treat the correlation as proof or change popup selectors/exit behavior on that assumption.
- **Some fresh-card failures are also unexplained:** Two attempts rebuild a target already on its native page, retry after 180 ms, then report failure; the next separate hover reuses the prepared card successfully. The replay cancellation counter lacks its rejection conditions. Loss of `:hover` after replacement, changed generation/ownership, or scroll suppression are possibilities; none is established by those exports.
- **Image observations now cover future entries:** Image resource counts increase from 75 to 499 and examined entries from 128 to 753, well below the 4,000-entry cap. This establishes additional image activity despite the saved browser timing history remaining at 250 entries. The observer includes native/popup artwork as well as grid images; these counts do not establish decode cost, layout shifts or a thumbnail bug. All 500 current first images remain complete with lazy/async hints. Opening Watched changes the eligible population, so this is not an identical-population rendering comparison.

Release **1.3.11 changes diagnostics only**. It preserves existing dwell, retry, native navigation, identity/generation guards, replay, release, geometry, image policy and grouping behavior:

- `hoverTiming` adds actual dwell callback count, total and maximum delay, including late timer delivery. `hoverInteraction` separates accepted/cancelled/rejected dwell intents, fresh replacements not matching `:hover`, rejected replay guards, and leaves before/after replay. A failure-only replay event reports scalar cancellation, route, connection, ownership, generation, hover/control and scroll-suppression conditions. The existing clone-update log adds its target's `:hover` state immediately after insertion. No geometry/style read is added to these probes.
- A pointer-leave event for an owned or pending hover records trusted/event type, target connection/hover state, elapsed time since native replay and the coordinate difference from replay. Missing coordinates remain unknown. Equal end coordinates do not establish that the pointer never moved between events. Destination inspection is capped at six ancestors and eight class tokens per ancestor; it exports only a tag and fixed grid/control/preview hints, never the class strings, text, IDs, URLs, HTML or React data. Preview hints are diagnostic cues, not a visibility measurement or a new event-handling rule. A missing hint cannot exclude a preview, especially when inspection is truncated. Failures cannot prevent native cleanup.
- `hoverFrames` uses one coalesced animation callback owner around accepted intent, preparation, successful replay and the start of a scroll burst. It records aggregate callback intervals and counts above 32/50/100 ms, with maxima by phase. An outstanding interval spanning a phase change remains measured and is labeled mixed; a late dwell timer cannot erase the preceding stall. The first sample includes scheduling-to-callback delay. Sampling stops at the next delivered callback after 600/6,000/2,000/1,000 ms respectively, or after 360 callbacks in a window; renewed windows share one owner. The route budget is 3,000 callbacks and cannot restart once exhausted. No frame logs, DOM search, geometry/style polling, image decode, network request or persistence write is added. Hidden-tab notification and route cleanup cancel the owner, and stale callbacks cannot write into a new owner. High-refresh displays can reach callback caps sooner; limits and stop reasons are exported.

These are callback-delivery measurements, not measured paint duration, GPU cost or script-specific CPU attribution. Browser scheduling/throttling and other page work can contribute. The [HTML animation-frame contract](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/imagebitmap-and-animations.html#animation-frames) provides callback delivery/cancellation; it does not make a callback timestamp a paint measurement. Sampling adds bounded numeric work while active and otherwise stops. No assistant browser inspection or authenticated Netflix request was performed.

All **265 deterministic tests pass**, including eleven new regressions for stationary/unknown pointer coordinates and bounded destination hints, diagnostic getter failures preserving native cleanup, fresh-target replay rejection, cancelled/rejected/completed and delayed dwell, phase/coalescing/timing bounds, delayed phase transitions retaining a stall, exhausted route budgets, hidden/obsolete route owners, API failures, and copied scalar snapshots. Existing hover retry, source identity, scroll suppression, grouping, collection, network and Undo tests remain passing. Syntax, matching increased release versions and whitespace checks pass. These fixtures validate diagnostics and retained behavior, not live popup smoothness.

Current priority is **(1)** reproduce the early-release/repeated-hover issue and inspect the new leave and replay-guard events; **(2)** distinguish native preparation latency and delayed dwell from callback stalls using the same updated export; **(3)** consider a focused hover change only after that evidence identifies its mechanism. Native traversal remains a known latency cost, but there is no evidence here supporting early release of already-clicked moves or a broader React bridge rewrite. Original **9** loading/sizing changes and **10** offscreen rendering remain conditional on request/decode/layout/paint evidence; original **1–8** remain implemented, with cap-two viewing overlap supported by the supplied live visits. The formatter idea remains unselected. Update/reload 1.3.11, reproduce the problematic hover and scroll soon after entry, then Copy Logs in that same visit; one export includes both preceding events and the bounded counters.

## Fresh-card admission and preview continuity — implemented in 1.3.12

The user supplied a 1.3.11 export copied at 18:38:31 on 2026-10-01. It contains 657 events for a 500-card visit in Firefox 157, viewport 1920 × 919, with seven columns and 72 native pages. This evidence resolves two questions left by the preceding diagnostic release; it does not establish that stock Netflix would behave the same way without the script.

- **Repeated hover has a concrete admission failure:** All eight rejected replay frames have `targetHovered: false`, with current token, active route, connected/owned target and grid, matching generation/active clone/title, no viewing-control hover and no scroll suppression. They are four intents with a rejected initial replay and rejected 180 ms retry. Separate subsequent hovers reuse those cards successfully. All 23 fresh replacements initially fail `:hover`, although many acquire it by their replay frame. Replacing the target and relying solely on its immediate CSS hover state is therefore insufficient for these first attempts.
- **Opening a preview ends the script bridge prematurely:** Sixteen recorded leaves transfer into a preview-hinted image, 417–433 ms after native replay; fourteen have zero coordinate displacement. The previous handler unconditionally sends a native exit and restores source geometry on those leaves. The log establishes transfer at those end coordinates, not a complete pointer trajectory, exact preview markup or proof of the animation cost. The cleanup nevertheless removes positioning while the pointer is inside the preview, so preserving an identified preview's owner is warranted.
- **Other latency remains distinct:** There are 28 preparations, 23 rebuilt targets, 136 skipped neighbors and 195 native moves. Summed movement is 7,948 ms, maximum individual move 337 ms and maximum successful preparation 1,234 ms. Queue wait totals one millisecond. Actual dwell delay averages about 123 ms and peaks at 175 ms; late dwell delivery does not explain a multi-second wait in this sample. Graft, alignment, replay and exit remain individually cheap. No warnings or recovery/restore failures appear.
- **Frame evidence has a coverage limit:** The diagnostic reaches its 3,000-callback budget after 12,540 ms of summed active intervals. The observed callback cadence is about 239 per second, not an independently measured display refresh rate. Its 88 ms maximum and fifteen intervals over 50 ms establish intermittent delivery gaps in that sampled portion; zero intervals over 100 ms cannot exclude later stalls, paint/compositor cost or gaps while sampling was inactive. It stopped before much of the later hovering. Increase bounded coverage to avoid that early cutoff on high-refresh systems.

The patch addresses the two coupled handoffs within the selected hover follow-up:

- **Admit a verified replacement:** Preserve a numeric preparation token on the fresh clone. Existing connection, grid ownership/visibility, generation, scroll/control, route/token and active-title/source guards remain. When that still-preparing replacement fails `:hover`, use the current physical pointer position, or the original intent coordinates when no current position is known, for one `document.elementFromPoint` check. Admit only a hit resolved to that exact grid card through the existing control-excluding delegation helper. Other cards, overlays, missing coordinates/APIs, hidden cards and obsolete intents fail closed. Once preparation markers are removed, this fallback expires. There is no extra frame, retry loop, rectangle-based assumption or relaxed native identity check. The [CSSOM View hit-test API](https://drafts.csswg.org/cssom-view/#dom-document-elementfrompoint) returns a hit-test target, not necessarily the topmost painted element; this check intentionally follows pointer targeting.
- **Keep one preview owner:** A trusted pointer-out from the active card can retain its existing native geometry and replay owner when the destination is inside a recognized Netflix preview wrapper/container with a matching title reference. Inspect at most eight ancestors and sixteen descendant link/tracking references; tracking strings over 4,096 characters are not decoded. Recognized tracking IDs and title/detail links identify series independently of their episode Play links; an episode ID alone cannot establish the series. A movie's matching `/watch/` link can identify it. No assumption that the active title must own every preview is made. Unidentified/foreign previews retain the previous release behavior and report a fixed rejection reason when a recognized container lacks identity. Local matching reads add no exported link, tracking graph, title/profile/authentication field or persisted record.
- **End the owner deliberately:** Movement among the preview image and controls retains alignment. Returning to the same still-valid card clears the temporary preview reference and keeps the native owner without another preparation/replay. Leaving the preview, entering a viewing action or moving elsewhere disposes of the owner and restores geometry. A single route-owned document pointer-out listener handles preview boundaries; existing physical pointer movement and document-mutation callbacks recover a missed boundary or removed root. Scroll, real resize, source replacement, filters/group changes, route cleanup and listener shutdown continue to release through the common native cleanup. Clear preview references before exit dispatch, preserve newer owners against stale events, and revalidate native identity before retaining a return to the card. No extra observer, popup polling, per-preview timer or native navigation work is added.
- **Verify the handoffs:** `hoverInteraction` counts replacement pointer checks, accepted/rejected hits and unavailable checks; guard logs include the last verified-hit result. `hoverLifecycle` counts preview transfers, returns, releases and rejections, with fixed reasons. `hoverTiming.preview` measures completed ownership intervals, and Copy Logs exposes a boolean `nativePreviewOwned`. Logs identify matched/rejected transfers and release reasons/durations. DOM references belong only to the one transient native hover owner and are cleared on release/return. Frame sampling retains its original time windows, coalescing, hidden/route cleanup, scalar-only callbacks and stop reasons, with a larger 12,000-callback route budget and 1,800-callback window cap. This is still finite sampling, not unlimited browsing coverage or paint attribution.

All **276 deterministic tests pass**, including eleven new regressions. Fixtures model delayed fresh-clone CSS hover with exact pointer targeting and verify first-attempt replay with one preparation and no retry; reject overlaid/foreign/control hits and cancelled/generation/route/filter/scroll changes; model the recorded 420 ms transfer while preserving the proxy through controls, returning to the card and real exits; cover bounded/missing/foreign identity, series episode links versus title/tracking identity and oversized tracking data; release on source/scroll/resize/listener teardown, removal and missed boundaries; reject stale/recycled owner returns and old preview events; and verify high-refresh sampling beyond the former 3,000-callback cap. Existing cancellation, source identity, clicked-move serialization, grouping, collection, network, Undo and diagnostic tests remain passing. Syntax, matching increased versions and whitespace checks pass. These prove the shipped lifecycle paths and bounds; matching the live Netflix preview markup and actual animation smoothness still require the user's check. No assistant browser inspection or authenticated Netflix request was performed. `context.md` was reviewed; its durable logging authorization remains unchanged.

Current priority is **first verify 1.3.12's first-hover response, positioning while the pointer enters preview controls, return-to-card and scrolling**, then examine one Copy Logs export from that visit if any delay/stutter remains. `replacementPointerAccepted` should explain rescued first attempts; `previewTransfers` should explain positioning retained past preview opening, while fixed rejection reasons reveal an unidentifiable preview rather than silently accepting one. Native page traversal remains a separate known wait and is the next hover assessment if it still dominates. Original **9** image-policy changes and **10** offscreen rendering remain conditional on appropriate request/decode/layout/paint evidence. Original **1–8** remain implemented; the formatter idea remains unselected. This patch does not claim to eliminate all Netflix animation costs or distant-title preparation waits.

## Remaining popup failures — live 1.3.12 evidence and 1.3.13 diagnostics

The user supplied an export copied at 19:25:27 on 2026-10-01, covering 814 events for 500 cards, seven columns and 72 native pages in Firefox 157 at 1920 × 919. They explicitly report that popups still need repeated hovering or take several seconds. The handoff fixes activate, but that report means the visible responsiveness problem remains open. Successful replay describes event dispatch, not Netflix accepting the hover or a popup appearing.

- **The preceding failures are absent in this sample:** All 24 replay attempts dispatch, with zero guard rejections, cancellations or failures. Two exact pointer checks rescue fresh replacements; 23 replacements still initially lack CSS hover. Twenty-two title-matched preview transfers retain positioning, with no rejected matches or failed exits/restores. Their transfers occur 415–433 ms after replay, average 422 ms. Eleven subsequently release on scrolling and eleven on leaving the preview. This validates matching the live preview structure and the recorded handoff paths; it does not prove that every attempted popup opened smoothly. No return-to-card transfer is captured in this visit.
- **Long preparations still explain some delays:** There are 262 acknowledged native moves, with no unchanged moves, queue wait totaling two milliseconds, aggregate move elapsed time 9,384 ms and a 276 ms maximum. Successful preparations range from 4 to 1,488 ms. One 71-to-8 request takes 63 direct moves, totaling 1,230 ms of movement and 1,286 ms overall preparation. Hawkins logical controls are deliberately treated as non-wrapping after the earlier boundary failure evidence; restoring the cyclic shortcut would restore that known risk. Two other requests cancel after 25 and 16 moves, before reaching their targets or replaying. The cancellation timestamps alone do not establish how long the pointer stayed on the card. The aggregate native waits include asynchronous acknowledgement and settling, not continuous CPU blocking.
- **Dispatch still leaves an observation gap:** Successful replays for hover sequences 19 and 20 have no corresponding preview-transfer event. An absence of pointer transfer cannot establish popup absence: a preview might exist elsewhere, leave the pointer on the card, or be interrupted. The old export cannot distinguish those possibilities. The existing quiet-period implementation already preserves genuine physical intent until quiet/dwell expires, with regression coverage; no new scroll-rearm fix is warranted from this log. Zero rejected dwell callbacks and a 122 ms maximum callback delay also give no evidence of a delayed dwell causing these multi-second waits.
- **Frame coverage now spans the visit's interactions:** There are 10,606 callback intervals over 46,346 ms of summed active sampling, with a 99 ms maximum, 149 intervals above 32 ms and 101 above 50 ms. The route budget is not exhausted; sampling stops at a completed window. Replay-phase maximum is 99 ms, preparation 91 ms, intent 90 ms, scroll 51 ms and mixed phase 92 ms. These are callback delivery gaps, not paint/GPU time or attribution to a particular script operation. Runs have different interactions and sampling coverage, so raw gap counts cannot establish a speed-up or regression between releases.
- **No renewed grouping or scan loop appears:** Only one full grouping pass updates the 500 controls; 25 sync calls consider 2,033 lightweight entries in total, and the last reason is a type filter. Viewing collection completes before the first recorded hover. No resize events, native recoveries or diagnostic failures appear. Current image pixels are complete; resource timings alone still do not establish decode/layout/paint cost. Image and offscreen-rendering policy remain measurement-gated.

Release **1.3.13** adds the missing evidence within the selected hover follow-up. At most the first 48 successful replay owners in a route receive one 900 ms timer. A title-matched pointer transfer completes the observation immediately and cancels that timer. Ordinary release reports its fixed reason before the check; hidden tabs and listener shutdown cancel it. For an owner still current at expiry, revalidate route/token, clone ownership/active title and mounted source identity before any hit test/search. Perform at most one `elementFromPoint` check and, only if it does not identify the matching preview, one preview-selector query. Inspect at most six returned roots, reusing the existing bounded title matcher; ordinary grid/native-source cards cannot establish preview presence even if they share legacy preview classes. A selector query itself is not a six-node browser traversal bound. No explicit rectangle/computed-style reads, per-frame DOM work, new observer, polling loop, network request, automatic retry or persistent data is added. The timer and temporary clone reference are cleared on completion; old owners/counters cannot affect a new hover or route.

`hoverPreview` exports sampling limits, completed/checked observations, pointer/search counts, probe duration, matched transfers/targets/other roots, no-root/unverified/early-release/invalid/hidden/API-failure outcomes and skipped-budget counts. `Hover preview presence observation` logs only numeric sequence/token/elapsed time and fixed scalar classifications. A matching root elsewhere proves a connected title-matched container, not that it is visible; hidden roots, unknown markup or bounded identity inspection can limit conclusions. Results describe the observation time: a later preview transfer can follow a no-root check. Delayed timer delivery is reported through actual time since replay. No title IDs, URLs, text, DOM/React objects, tracking payloads or profile/authentication data are exported by the new observation.

`hoverTiming.acknowledgement` measures the existing explicit wait after a click; `settlement` measures the subsequent stabilization. Both include asynchronous elapsed time, remain nested within `move`, and must not be summed with `move` as independent costs. They exclude the click's preceding synchronous work and do not attribute CPU or paint. Existing native acknowledgement/settlement serialization, timeouts, style restoration, direct routing, hover retries and popup behavior remain unchanged.

All **284 deterministic tests pass**, including eight new regressions for the delayed single check and expired ownership; early matched-transfer cancellation; pointer versus elsewhere presence without adopting/changing a preview; identity inspection limits and scalar-only output; excluding ordinary cards that share legacy preview classes; scroll/resize/source/route/hidden/listener cleanup; stale callbacks and recycled source/route owners; API/logger failures preserving native cleanup; and the finite route replay budget. The existing clicked-move test now separately verifies 80 ms acknowledgement and 40 ms settlement while queued moves remain serialized. Syntax, matching increased versions and whitespace checks pass. No assistant browser inspection or authenticated Netflix request was performed. `context.md` was reviewed without changing its durable logging authorization.

Current priority is **(1)** use one 1.3.13 export after holding over a misbehaving card for roughly three seconds to distinguish successful replay with no matching preview, an existing preview elsewhere, or earlier release; **(2)** use acknowledgement/settlement timings to select a focused reduction of long native preparation, preserving already-clicked movement safety; **(3)** pursue remaining animation/scroll gaps only with evidence identifying the relevant work. Original **9–10** remain conditional; **1–8** remain implemented. The formatter idea remains unselected. This is a diagnostic release, not a claim that the user's remaining popup failures are fixed.

## Section-dependent hover delay — live 1.3.13 evidence and 1.3.14

The user supplied an export copied at 19:58:33 on 2026-10-01 and reports improved responsiveness, with the main legacy My List still slower than Watched / Caught up, and Netflix's native list a little faster again. The export contains 832 events for 500 cards, seven columns and 72 native pages in Firefox 157 at 1920 × 919. Both script sections use the same native preparation and replay functions; the preceding logs do not label the section and contain no timed native-list comparison. A different preparation implementation for the two sections is not established.

- **Recorded preview reliability improved:** All 24 replay attempts dispatch and have a title-matched preview transfer, with zero guard rejections, cancelled/failed replays or rejected preview matches. Transfers occur 415–439 ms after replay. All 24 preview observations finish on the matching transfer, cancelling their delayed timers before a hit test or selector search; observation checks, pointer checks and root searches are all zero. These recorded attempts no longer show the two missing transfers from the preceding visit. The sample does not establish responsiveness for every possible title or smooth painting.
- **Preparation still dominates the long waits:** There are 261 native moves totaling 9,087 ms. Acknowledgement accounts for 6,381 ms (about 70%) and subsequent settlement 2,530 ms (about 28%); these nested asynchronous waits are not independent CPU measurements and must not be added to the move total. Successful preparations take 5–1,412 ms. Acknowledgement reaches 176 ms on a step. Early traversals contain slow steps, whereas later traversals mostly take roughly 19–27 ms per step, including a 53-step return. Warm native state or visit order could explain part of the user's section comparison, but the unlabelled visit does not prove it. Native hovering avoids the userscript's native-page preparation; its actual latency was not measured here.
- **No renewed background-work flood appears:** There is one full grouping pass and 500 control updates; 23 group syncs consider 1,425 lightweight entries in total. Viewing requests finish before the first recorded hover. No resize events, native recovery attempts or diagnostic failures occur. Frame sampling spans 11,538 callbacks with an 83 ms maximum and 91 intervals above 50 ms; it does not identify CPU, image decoding, paint or GPU causes. Image and offscreen-rendering changes still lack their required attribution.

Release **1.3.14** reduces repeated inspections in the selected native-hover follow-up. An already-clicked logical hover move now uses one transient observer on the native scroller, covering descendant child changes and `href`, `style`, `class` and `tabindex` attributes through the [DOM mutation-observer contract](https://dom.spec.whatwg.org/#interface-mutationobserver). A first animation frame covers synchronous click updates; subsequent mutation notifications coalesce into one frame before reading native content and geometry. Quiet animation frames perform no acknowledgement inspection. One 80 ms fallback wake covers computed visibility changes or updates outside the observed attributes. The original three-second timeout, signature/page identity mapping, direct Hawkins routing, clicked-move serialization, subsequent settlement and temporary style restoration remain in force. This reduces inspection work while waiting; it does not remove the native page traversal itself. An update with no observed notification can wait up to the fallback interval, so a visible speed-up must be verified in the user's next visit.

The observer, pending frame and timer are released after acknowledgement, timeout, cancellation handling or route failure. Cancellation stops scheduling new signal frames and retains coarse acknowledgement of the already-clicked move before the queue can proceed. Missing or failed observer APIs fall back to the previous frame-based acknowledgement. Cleanup failures cannot strand a signal waiter; closed callbacks and captured diagnostic owners cannot affect replacement route counters. Non-hover collection retains its previous polling behavior. No persistent observer, automatic popup retry, HTTP request, whole-grid operation or image/rendering policy change is added.

`goToPage` now reads the selected page once per loop iteration instead of twice, preserving navigation, cancellation and loop-guard behavior. `hoverLifecycle` counts avoided duplicate page reads, actual logical acknowledgement inspections, observer starts/notifications, signal frames, fallback wakes and unavailable/failed signal APIs. Inspection counts cover this wait, not every native DOM read elsewhere in preparation. Existing hover request/result/reuse events gain a fixed `main` or `watched` group label, and preparation requests report direct page distance. `hoverTiming.mainPreparation` and `watchedPreparation` separately aggregate successful preparation counts/elapsed times/maxima, including immediate reuse and excluding the preceding intent dwell. Page distance and existing move timings allow section comparisons to account for traversal work; later native-preview time is separate. New diagnostics add only scalar counters and fields to existing events, without new title/profile data or per-card logging.

All **295 deterministic tests pass**, including eleven new regressions covering mutation coalescing with zero quiet-frame DOM reads; synchronous and notification-free updates; timeout cleanup; unsupported/failed observer, frame and timer APIs; cancellation with serialized acknowledgement; obsolete routes and closed callbacks; cleanup API failures; unchanged non-hover collection; real signal acknowledgement followed by deferred settlement and queued newer/obsolete moves; duplicate page-read removal with guard/cancellation preservation; and equal main/watched preparation paths with separate group timings. Existing source identity, preview ownership, scrolling, collection, grouping, network and Undo checks remain passing. Syntax, matching increased versions and whitespace checks pass. These fixtures verify work counts and lifecycle behavior, not a measured browser speed-up. No assistant browser inspection or authenticated Netflix request was performed. `context.md` was reviewed; no durable preference changed.

Current priority is **(1)** verify 1.3.14 hover response in both sections and inspect one Copy Logs export for the new section timings, page distances, logical inspection/signal counts and native acknowledgement/settlement totals; **(2)** use that evidence to decide whether another bounded preparation change is justified; **(3)** pursue remaining animation/scroll gaps only with attributable evidence. Section timing aggregates alone cannot distinguish different page distances or warmed native state; matching traversal distance and visit order matters. Original **1–8** remain implemented, **9–10** remain conditional on request/decode/layout/paint evidence, and the formatter idea remains unselected. The user's remaining latency report is open pending live verification; neither the full performance task nor native preparation latency is declared solved.

## Hover after scrolling — live 1.3.14 assessment

The user supplied an export copied at 20:35:33 on 2026-10-01, containing 727 events for 500 cards in Firefox 157 at 1920 × 919. They report that ordinary hovering works fine, while hovering after scrolling seems less reactive, explicitly identifying this as one observation. This follow-up is analysis only: the userscript remains at **1.3.14**, without a new behavior change or diagnostic release. No assistant browser inspection or authenticated Netflix request was performed.

- **Post-scroll rearming is the next focused question.** `handleTargetScroll` cancels pending intent, sets `hoverNeedsPointerMove` and, once per burst, exits the active native hover and releases the existing grafts. Both captured `wheel` and `scroll` events update the quiet deadline. A boundary-only pointer entry is suppressed until physical pointer coordinates change; a stationary card under the pointer does not automatically reopen when scrolling stops. A genuine movement can queue intent during the quiet period, with a timer delayed by the larger of the normal 120 ms dwell and the remaining 180 ms scroll quiet period. Further scrolling cancels that timer again. The 33 completed dwell samples average 131.2 ms and reach 179 ms, with zero rejected dwell callbacks; this does not establish a multi-second dwell stall. It also does not measure time spent waiting for the first accepted physical movement or cancelled timers. The current log lacks per-request time since the last scroll, quiet-period versus movement suppression counts, and scroll/leave/supersession causes for every token invalidation. The code can explain a perceived post-scroll pause, but the exact observed transition cannot be reconstructed from this export.
- **Long native preparation remains independently visible.** There are 33 preparation attempts and 23 successful replays. Ten attempts cancel after token changes; four have explicit trusted card-leave events, while the other six lack an exported cause for their invalidation. Cancelled preparations last roughly 44–287 ms from request to cancellation and stop before replay; they do not establish ten failed popups or an endless background scan. There are 198 native moves totaling 8,659 ms, with 6,396 ms acknowledgement and 2,123 ms settlement nested within that total. Queue waiting is zero. Successful preparation reaches 1,909 ms; the corresponding 22 moves account for 1,892 ms. Other long successful preparations take 728, 962, 975 and 1,048 ms. A post-scroll jump to another part of the grid can require these distant native pages even after intent has been accepted. Already-clicked cancellation acknowledgement remains serialized, as designed.
- **Section labels clarify the earlier comparison without establishing its cause.** Main-list successes number 17, averaging 346.5 ms with a 47 ms median and 1,909 ms maximum. Watched successes number six, averaging 269.5 ms with a 246 ms median and 638 ms maximum. Main successes make 77 native moves in 5,726 ms (74.4 ms per step); watched successes make 78 in 1,525 ms (19.6 ms per step). Thus watched does not have uniformly shorter preparations or less traversal: it averages 13 pages per success against main's 4.5. Slow main outliers raise its mean, and its early/native steps are often slower. Revisited native content or visit order remains a plausible explanation because both sections share the same preparation path and watched is visited later. These different title/page histories do not prove that warming, scrolling, a section, image loading or 1.3.14 itself causes the difference. There is still no timed native-list control or matched prior-release visit.
- **Successful replay still reliably reaches the recorded preview.** All 23 replay attempts dispatch without guard rejection or failure. Twenty-one title-matched transfers occur 413–428 ms after replay. The other two observations end on a trusted pointer leave 193 ms after replay and a scroll two milliseconds after replay, respectively; no persistent undispatched or missing-preview attempt is captured. The delayed presence probes perform zero checks, hit tests or searches. Seven exact pointer checks accept newly replaced cards. Matching ownership does not measure painting smoothness, and the native preview delay follows both the script dwell and any preparation.
- **The new wait mechanism is active, with no API failures.** All 198 logical moves create their transient observer; 674 acknowledgement inspections, 729 notification callbacks, 659 signal frames and eight fallback wakes are recorded. There are zero unsupported/failed signal APIs, and 198 duplicate selected-page reads are avoided. These totals include cancelled-move acknowledgement. Prior releases did not count equivalent inspections, so this visit cannot quantify their reduction or prove a browser speed-up. This sample's 122 ms maximum frame interval and one interval above 100 ms remain callback-gap observations, not attribution of CPU/paint work to the signal helper.
- **Background work remains bounded.** One full group sync updates the 500 controls; 23 syncs consider 1,425 entries, with 11 active-hover preservations and zero group-triggered cancellations. Viewing collection finishes at 20:34:43.105, during the first few hovers rather than before all hovering, with 23 successful requests, peak overlap two and zero failures/rate limits. The scan does not keep running through later browsing. No resize events, native recovery attempts, failed alignment restores, warnings or errors occur. The Copy Logs thumbnail geometry sample has ready pixels for all 24 sampled images and takes six milliseconds overall; it cannot establish request-time position, decoding or paint causes.

Revised priority: **(1)** distinguish post-scroll quiet/movement gating from preparation using bounded counters and fields on accepted hover request/result events, and record explicit cancellation causes; **(2)** then assess a narrowly scoped scroll-rearm adjustment that preserves cancellation during actual scrolling and avoids stationary scroll-triggered popups; **(3)** continue native preparation work only with timing evidence separating observed notifications, fallback detection and slow native rendering. Further lowering timeouts, retaining stale React grafts or enabling cyclic Hawkins shortcuts is not justified by this sample. New logging is a valid authorized investigation tool; this assessment records what is missing without silently choosing a behavior change. Original **1–8** remain implemented; **9–10** retain their image/rendering measurement gates, and the formatter idea remains unselected. `context.md` was reviewed with no durable preference change. Documentation diff and whitespace checks suffice for this analysis-only follow-up; runtime tests were not rerun for an unchanged userscript.

## Post-scroll intent and interruption diagnostics — implemented in 1.3.15

The next selected step completes the logging gap identified in the 1.3.14 assessment. It measures existing behavior without changing the 120 ms normal dwell, 180 ms scroll quiet period, requirement for physical movement after scrolling, native page routing, clicked-move acknowledgement/settlement, preview ownership or retry policy. No assistant browser inspection or authenticated Netflix request was performed.

`performanceWork.hoverScroll` adds flat route-owned counters for captured wheel/scroll/other notifications, boundary entries suppressed by the quiet deadline and/or movement requirement, unchanged trusted pointer coordinates while movement is required, physical/dwell rearming, and intents queued during quiet or while awaiting movement. Event counts describe received notifications, not unique gestures or proof that the page moved. The quiet and movement suppression counts can both increase for one rejected boundary entry. No DOM search, geometry/style read, new listener, timer, observer or request is added to these event paths; high-frequency callbacks add scalar counter work without a new event log.

Accepted intent carries a scalar snapshot into the existing hover preparation request/reuse/result events: whether physical movement queued it, time since scroll at queue and activation, remaining quiet time at queue, the movement-required flag, scheduled dwell and actual elapsed dwell. The two time constraints overlap through the existing maximum-delay rule, rather than being added together. This snapshot is made when the existing dwell callback accepts the card; it does not include later native preparation or Netflix preview time. Direct activation paths without a queued intent report `null` instead of inventing dwell data. Cancelled pending timers record their partial elapsed dwell and one fixed cause; they add no per-cancellation event. One transient scalar pending owner is disposed on cancellation or timer delivery. Copy Logs runtime snapshots also expose the current movement-required flag, quiet time remaining, age of the latest scroll notification, pending-intent/preparation booleans and last notification kind. This reveals a still-waiting state at copy time without polling it.

Every existing hover-token increment now passes through the same increment operation with a fixed cancellation cause. An active native preparation owns only sequence/token/group/start time, the scalar intent snapshot and captured diagnostic counters. Its first token invalidation disposes that diagnostic owner and counts the interruption before any logger work. At most **48** such interruptions per route emit `Hover preparation interrupted`, reporting the original sequence/token, new token, group, cause, elapsed preparation time, time since scroll and optional intent snapshot. Subsequent invalidations cannot replace its first cause or emit another interruption for it. Aggregate counters continue after the log cap and count skipped events. Causes distinguish scroll, pointer leave, superseding hover, resize, viewing-group changes, source changes/recovery, route teardown, viewing controls, outside-grid movement, preview release and a fixed `other` fallback; arbitrary reason text is excluded. These causes describe script invalidation, not Netflix rejecting a preview. The preparation owner begins once activation owns a hover token; a cancelled responsive-refresh wait before that point is not a token-owned preparation interruption.

Older asynchronous completion cannot clear a newer diagnostic owner, and captured old-route counters cannot update new-route statistics. Successful/failed completion clears its scalar owner in the existing `finally`; cancelled preparation disposes it immediately, while native acknowledgement and cleanup continue normally. The diagnostic retains no source/clone/event/title/profile reference and creates no journal of title activity. Logger failure cannot prevent the token increment, cancellation, native exit or cleanup. This patch supplies causal evidence; it does not claim a visible speed-up or a completed scroll-rearm fix.

All **305 deterministic tests pass**, including ten new regressions covering quiet/movement/unchanged-coordinate distinctions; early physical intent with the existing overlapping delay; later movement and ordinary dwell with no extra scheduling; one partial-dwell cause through scroll/leave/outside/supersession/resize paths; scalar intent propagation into real preparation events; first interruption causes before obsolete replay cleanup; old/new preparation and route ownership; the 48-event limit and fixed anonymous fields; failed logging with preserved cancellation cleanup; superseding-card and viewing-control causes; and copied scalar runtime/counter snapshots without native reads. Existing actual hover/preview, clicked-move serialization, geometry restoration, collection, grouping, network and Undo coverage remains passing. Syntax, increased matching metadata/internal versions and whitespace checks pass. `context.md` was reviewed without a durable preference change.

Current ordered work: **(1)** validate these diagnostics in one user-supplied 1.3.15 export after reproducing the less-reactive post-scroll hover; **(2)** select a bounded scroll-rearm change only if the recorded quiet/movement/interruption state supports it; **(3)** continue native preparation or animation work according to the measured mechanism. The export should include existing page distances/move timings and the new intent/cancellation fields, so a long native traversal is not mistaken for quiet-period delay. ## Post-scroll diagnostics — live 1.3.15 assessment

The supplied export was copied at **21:15:25 on 2026-10-01**, with 614 events, 500 cards, eight columns and 63 native pages in Firefox 157 at 2560 × 1279. The attachment contains the diagnostic export without an additional plain-text request or a labelled reproduction sequence. This completes the selected diagnostic assessment; the userscript remains **1.3.15**. No assistant browser inspection or authenticated Netflix request was performed.

- **Accepted post-scroll intent is not stuck in the timer.** All 22 completed dwell callbacks are accepted, averaging 122.5 ms with a 155 ms maximum and zero dwell rejections. Six preparations follow physical movement queued during the quiet period. They begin 181–287 ms after the latest scroll notification, with the existing dwell/quiet constraints overlapping as intended; four dispatch successfully, one later cancels on renewed scrolling and one on card leave. There are 86 queued intents and 64 cancellations: 54 scroll, nine pointer leave and one viewing control. The 65 intents queued during quiet/while movement is required include subsequently cancelled intents, rather than 65 completed preparations. Received events total 107 wheel and 725 scroll notifications. These totals do not distinguish gestures, intentional continued scrolling or one user's intended final card, so preserving a cancelled target or automatically reopening a stationary target is not selected from the counts alone. Movement suppression occurs 37 times and quiet suppression 36 times; those overlapping counters must not be added. At copy time the quiet deadline is expired, movement is no longer required and neither intent nor preparation is pending.
- **Every token-owned preparation interruption now has a cause.** All eight interruptions are logged below the 48-event limit: five card leaves, two scrolls and one resize refresh. There are no unknown, group, source, route or supersession causes in this visit. Three card-leave interruptions occur after about 1.1–1.4 seconds of preparation, including native traversal already in progress. The log establishes a cancelled attempt before replay, not why the user left that card or whether continued waiting would have been acceptable. The two scroll interruptions occur 98 and 174 ms after preparation starts. No new popup should be replayed for those obsolete targets.
- **One concrete startup interruption deserves a bounded fix.** The first preparation starts at 21:14:34.741 and is invalidated 21 ms later by the initial `ResizeObserver` refresh, which lasts 243 ms. Its before/after layouts retain eight columns, 295 px cards, 2,416 px grid width, 72 px left/padding, 2,560 px scroller width and the same gaps/page shape. The changed field is native `scrollerHeight`, from 173.6 px to 1 px. Source inspection confirms that `responsiveLayoutMatches` treats that field as a real geometry change, while responsive signatures and applied grid checks do not require a height change; the refresh then calls `cancelResizeHover`. This is consistent with parking the hidden carousel invalidating the first hover despite stable displayed grid geometry. A follow-up should recognize a height-only transition caused by the owned parked source when grid geometry and mapping remain valid, with explicit diagnostics and regressions. Actual viewport/zoom changes, unparked source-height changes, width/gap/clipping changes and stale page mapping must retain their cancellation/refresh checks. This explains a startup edge case, not all post-scroll latency. No such behavior change is included in this assessment.
- **Native page traversal remains the main recorded delay.** The 187 serialized moves total 8,119 ms, including 6,030 ms acknowledgement and 1,944 ms settlement. Those nested elapsed waits are not additional CPU totals. Queue wait totals ten milliseconds with a seven-millisecond maximum; there are no move timeouts, API failures or boundary detours. Successful main preparations number 12, averaging 218.8 ms and reaching 1,021 ms; watched preparations number two, taking 936 and 389 ms. The watched successes traverse 46 and 19 pages, respectively, so this export does not show watched preparation to be inherently faster. Early cold traversal contains substantially slower steps than later traversal, but this is an observation about visit order, not proof of a cache or image-decoding cause. Graft totals ten milliseconds, alignment seven, replay four and exit two. Reducing dwell alone would leave both traversal and the separate native preview delay.
- **Successful dispatch still reaches matching previews reliably in this sample.** All 14 replay attempts dispatch without rejection/failure; 13 title-matched transfers occur 414–433 ms after replay. The remaining observation releases on a trusted pointer leave 143–144 ms after replay with a 150.5 px coordinate difference, before the normal transfer interval. It is not a recorded popup that stayed missing while the same hover was retained. Four replacement-card pointer checks accept the requested card, with zero rejected or unavailable checks. All preview observations finish without delayed hit tests or root searches. Matching ownership does not measure painting smoothness or establish complete reliability for unrecorded attempts.
- **Background work is still bounded; rendering attribution remains open.** Viewing collection completes in 1,783 ms with 23 successful requests, concurrency two and no failures/rate limits/aborts. There is one full grouping pass, 500 control updates, 196 moves into watched/caught up, 18 active-hover preservations and zero grouping cancellations. No later scan, source recovery, alignment restore failure, warning or error appears. Logical movement records 187 observer starts, 641 acknowledgement inspections, 681 mutation notifications, 629 signal frames and ten fallback wakes, with no unsupported/failed APIs. Frame diagnostics reach a 101 ms maximum with 74 callback intervals above 50 ms; this does not identify JavaScript, decode, layout or paint cost. All 500 thumbnail first images have ready pixels; image resource totals include native and popup images and still cannot establish a loading-policy defect. The changed viewport/column count and different traversal history prevent an aggregate before/after speed-up claim.

Updated ordered follow-up: **(1)** the 1.3.15 diagnostic validation is complete; **(2)** recommend the narrowly scoped parked-height startup fix as the next concrete implementation, retaining true resize/remap guards and adding verification counters; **(3)** continue native-preparation analysis as the principal latency work, preserving clicked-move serialization and title identity; **(4)** revisit scroll rearming only with evidence that an intended stationary final card is lost after real movement, rather than counts of cancellation during ongoing scrolling. A new general reduction of the quiet/dwell deadlines is not supported by this export. Original **1–8** remain implemented; **9–10** retain their image/rendering measurement gates, and the formatter idea remains unselected. `context.md` was reviewed with no durable preference change. ## Startup hover preservation and closer traversal investigation — 1.3.16

The user selected the targeted startup cancellation fix and a closer investigation of native traversal. Release **1.3.16 implements the startup fix** and adds inexpensive verification data. Traversal routing, move waits, style suppression/restoration, native identity checks, cancellation, dwell/quiet periods and preview ownership remain unchanged; the traversal work below is investigation rather than an unselected optimization. No assistant browser inspection or authenticated Netflix request was performed.

An observer check recognizes a collapse from a previously taller native source to its owned one-pixel hidden standby height only when the source is marked parked, its section is explicitly hidden, the published grid's viewport signature still matches, every other measured layout field matches, the responsive signature is unchanged, applied grid width/left/columns match and logical page mapping is valid. Empty/unparked/visible sources, non-observer reasons, expansion or other height changes, missing/stale viewport baselines, real geometry/clipping changes and stale mapping retain the previous refresh path. The complete grid publication now establishes the viewport baseline before registering its observer. Qualifying checks update the stored source height and preserve the existing hover/token instead of starting a 243 ms responsive refresh and cancelling the first preparation. Real reported viewport/zoom changes still cancel immediately; a subsequent unchanged observer check cannot revive that cancelled intent. This is a narrow correction to the startup case recorded in 1.3.15, not a claim that distant-title preparation is instant.

Copy Logs adds route-owned `resize.parkedHeightChangesIgnored` and `parkedHeightHoverPreserved` counters. Preservation includes an accepted native preparation in progress, as well as pending or active hover. Only the first qualifying collapse per route emits the existing responsive-no-shape-change event, with scalar previous/current height, signature, reason and preservation result; later qualifying transitions remain counted without another event. Ordinary unchanged observer notifications do not count as another collapse. Logger failure cannot cancel the preserved hover or strand scheduling, and increments the existing diagnostic-failure counter. The existing coalesced check gains scalar/attribute comparisons and a viewport-signature read, without another native rectangle/computed-style read, timer, listener, observer, request or retained diagnostic DOM reference.

The closer source review distinguishes the remaining options:

- **Repeated work within a journey is the best next bounded candidate.** Each ordinary `moveOnePage` applies and restores native animation suppression and forces a track layout at both boundaries. After logical page acknowledgement it still runs `waitForScriptMoveSettle`, requiring stable content and a further paint opportunity, on every intermediate page. Collection already has a shared fast mode, but that mode assumes its caller supplies stabilization; merely enabling its class for hover traversal would omit necessary final/cancelled-move settlement. A future patch should own suppression across one serialized journey and settle the final requested source, with explicit handling for an interrupted already-clicked move, route/source replacement, newer queued hovers and deferred native style writes. Cancellation must never release the native queue or restore styles before an outstanding move can finish safely. This can reduce repeated waits/flushes without guessing the title or shortening acknowledgement timeouts. The supplied aggregate attributes about 24% of movement elapsed time to settlement and 74% to acknowledgement; those are nested elapsed phases, not CPU measurements or a promised 24% reduction in whole-popup latency. Forced-layout cost is not independently measured.
- **No verified direct jump exists in the current bridge.** `goToPage` uses native left/right controls and already stops without movement when the requested page is current. The observed Hawkins logical path does not provide a verified page-selector jump, and its non-wrapping boundary contract rules out reinstating cyclic shortcuts. Calling guessed private React callbacks or mutating native carousel indices would need a proven current contract and title/remount/undo/resize validation. Neither repository source nor the supplied exports establishes that contract. Such a jump is not implemented or presented as a guaranteed solution.
- **Mounted buffers and cached grafts cannot yet replace active-source validation.** The export has only 26 native slots and eight current cards for 500 titles, so most distant titles require another native window. `findMountedSourceSlot` can inspect non-current buffers, but `findActiveSourceSlot`, alignment and replay deliberately require the current source. Adjacent-buffer reuse might avoid nearby movement, but offscreen presence alone does not establish working popup handlers/placement. Grafts copy private props and fiber return chains that belong to a live source; retaining them after recycling or across scroll could reintroduce stale/wrong-title or scroll-triggered popups. No speculative cache, buffer fast path, guessed title substitution or background prewarming is added. The existing cheap ready-source reuse remains.

To resolve which intermediate waits are actually slow, each existing hover `Carousel move completed` event now includes `acknowledgementMs` and `settlementMs` alongside its total elapsed time and token. These reuse the durations already calculated for route-owned aggregate timing; they add no timing calls, DOM reads, new events or scheduling. If the captured timing owner became obsolete, the phase value is `null`, rather than attributing a stale duration to the new route. Non-hover collection does not gain the fields. The next export can correlate cold/warm steps and long/cancelled journeys by token instead of assuming a section or phase caused an aggregate difference. The phases are contained in the total, with style setup/restoration and other work outside them; they must not be added to the total.

All **312 deterministic tests pass**. Seven new regressions cover active/pending/preparing hover preservation and bounded logs, negative source/viewport/layout/mapping/clipping cases, reason restrictions and coalesced observations, immediate real viewport cancellation, obsolete checks, logger failure, and an actual first preparation that survives the observer check and dispatches native replay. Existing publication coverage verifies the new grid's viewport baseline; serialized-click coverage verifies distinct per-move acknowledgement/settlement values, and timing-owner coverage verifies no elapsed value is returned for obsolete counters. Prior native style restoration, clicked-move serialization, hover/preview identity, resize/remapping, scrolling, grouping, collection and Undo tests remain passing. Syntax, increased matching metadata/internal versions, diff review and whitespace checks pass. `context.md` was reviewed without a durable preference change. These establish control flow and bounded operation counts; visible responsiveness and browser frame-time improvement remain user-owned verification.

Current order: **(1)** startup preservation is implemented; validate 1.3.16 on list entry using the new resize counters/event; **(2)** evaluate one journey-level suppression/final-settlement patch using the new per-move phase fields, preserving acknowledgement and cancellation ownership; **(3)** keep direct jumping and buffer/graft reuse conditional on a verified native contract; **(4)** revisit scroll rearming only if an intended final hover is demonstrated lost after actual movement. Original **1–8** remain implemented, **9–10** retain their image/rendering measurement gates, and the formatter idea remains unselected. The broader latency/smoothness objective remains open.

## No perceived speed change — live 1.3.16 validation

The user reports **no noticeable change** and supplies an export copied at 21:56:10 on 2026-10-01, with 797 events for 500 cards, eight columns and 63 native pages in Firefox 157 at 2560 × 1279. This is validation of the selected startup patch and further assessment of traversal. The script remains **1.3.16**; the larger traversal optimization has not been selected for implementation. No assistant browser inspection or authenticated Netflix request was performed.

**The startup interruption is corrected in this visit.** The first preparation begins at 21:55:22.983; at 21:55:23.002 the observer explicitly records an ignored owned-height collapse with `hoverPreserved: true`, and the same preparation completes successfully in 30 ms. Its matching preview transfers about 420 ms after replay. Copy Logs reports `parkedHeightChangesIgnored: 1`, `parkedHeightHoverPreserved: 1`, two unchanged checks, zero responsive refreshes and zero resize hover/preparation cancellations. This validates preservation of the first hover, without contradicting the user's overall responsiveness report or claiming a general speed-up.

**Long native preparation still explains a visible multi-second wait.** All 34 completed dwell callbacks are accepted, averaging 124.9 ms with a 176 ms maximum and zero rejections. There are 31 dispatched replays without replay failure/guard rejection; 25 reach matching preview transfers 414–430 ms later. Six observations end early: five trusted pointer leaves with changed coordinates and one scroll, all 53–239 ms after replay. Three native preparations cancel before replay, from two leaves and one scroll; no cause is unknown or resize-related. The slowest successful preparation takes 1,588 ms, following 160 ms dwell and preceding a 429 ms matching-preview transfer: approximately **2.18 seconds from accepted intent to recorded preview transfer**. This measures neither time before accepted intent nor paint completion. Another success takes 1,513 ms before the separate native preview delay. Graft/alignment/replay remain small, totaling 20/21/6 ms across the visit.

**The new per-move fields support a specific next optimization rather than arbitrary timeout reductions.** The 195 moves total 8,357 ms, with 6,075 ms acknowledgement and 2,137 ms settlement nested within that total (about 73% and 26%). Queue wait totals only two milliseconds. Representative preparations, correlated by their hover token, show where the delay differs:

| Preparation sequence | Native moves | Preparation ms | Acknowledgement sum ms | Settlement sum ms | Preview result |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | --- |
| 13 | 16 | 1,588 | 1,241 | 195 | Matching transfer |
| 16 | 13 | 1,513 | 1,084 | 298 | Matching transfer |
| 25 | 22 | 451 | 199 | 218 | Matching transfer |
| 27 | 41 | 845 | 384 | 404 | Pointer leave before transfer |

On later fast traversals, repeated settlement is roughly half the movement time: the 22-step success spends 218 ms settling, and the 41-step preparation spends 404 ms. On the slow 16-step success, acknowledgement is dominant and settlement is only 195 ms. Thus a journey-level suppression/final-settlement patch has a concrete potential saving on repeated waits, but cannot by itself remove the 1,241 ms native-update acknowledgement or the roughly 420 ms preview delay. The phase sums are subsets of movement/preparation elapsed time, not extra CPU totals; source validation, final mounting, styles and other work remain outside them. For cancelled preparations, an already-clicked move may complete after the recorded interruption, so its full movement duration must not be treated as time before cancellation. Cold/warm behavior remains an observation about visit order, not established cache or rendering attribution.

The next implementation candidate should keep suppression owned across the journey, preserve serialization of outstanding clicks and still settle/validate the final source. It must also handle incomplete intermediate native acknowledgement/hydration, deferred native writes, cancellation, route/source replacement and newer queued hovers; merely toggling the existing collection fast-mode class would not establish those guarantees. Retain fallback settling when the next move cannot safely start. Native Hawkins indicators remain unavailable in the observed profile, so no direct-jump mechanism is established. The next live acceptance criteria should include fewer settlement waits/style restorations per multi-page request, exact title identity, unchanged safe cancellation/cleanup, and separate preparation/preview timing rather than a claim of instant native hovering.

Background work still finishes: one full grouping pass and 500 control updates; viewing collection takes 1,705 ms with 23 successful requests, overlap two and zero failures/rate limits/aborts. Logical-move observation has no unsupported/failed APIs, and there are no diagnostic failures, source-recovery attempts or alignment-restore failures. The existing frame sample reaches 112 ms, with 108 gaps above 50 ms; it still does not attribute animation stutter to script CPU, native rendering, decode or paint. The user's broader performance concern remains open. Startup verification is now **complete for this recorded visit**; next priority is the bounded traversal patch described above, followed by remaining native-update/animation attribution if needed. Original **1–8** remain implemented, **9–10** keep their measurement gates, and the formatter idea remains unselected. `context.md` was reviewed without a durable preference change.
