## Preload information before hover — discussion

The user asks whether information behind every visible card could be loaded with the initial grid so later hovering avoids another carousel journey. This is a design discussion, not selection of a new preload mechanism or popup implementation. Source review confirms that `buildGraphqlMyListItems` retains compact title identity/link, title text, artwork, logical position and a shared native DOM template. `createItemClone` builds the displayed card from those records; viewing status is collected separately. A complete visible grid therefore establishes card markup/artwork readiness, not a complete cached popup dataset or a running native popup for each title. Additional information might exist in Netflix's responses/cache; the current parser does not establish or retain everything needed for a full popup.

The current hover bridge needs more than information: `graftTreeToClone` copies private React props and fiber return chains from the requested live native card, and activation/alignment/replay validate that same active source. Visiting every title early could warm native data, but would not by itself keep every source or copied handler valid after Netflix recycles its virtual window. Caching serializable title data is a separate mechanism from retaining DOM/fiber/handler references. No permanent full-list live-native-card cache or promise that a preload scan removes later traversal is justified by the current contract.

Preloading usable title metadata remains a plausible investigation, preferably using existing response data first and measuring any additional bounded background requests, cache hits/misses, readiness at hover and startup cost. The user explicitly rejects viewport-based priority: every title should have equal loading priority, including titles outside the viewport or currently filtered/collapsed. A neutral queue with bounded background batches can preserve the quick grid display without favoring visible/nearby cards. The user accepts showing cached details immediately and refreshing them later; strict live freshness per hover is unnecessary for normally stable title information. No specific refresh interval or longer startup target has been selected. More data readiness may help native rendering, but cannot alone eliminate the live-source requirement in the current bridge.

To remove that dependency, either verify a way to open Netflix's native popup from title data without requiring the carousel's active card, or provide a script-owned cached-information popup. The former has no proven current contract; the latter can render available data directly but requires defining the displayed fields, appearance, supported actions and any differences from Netflix's native video preview/buttons. The user accepts cached display with later refresh in principle, including for a script-rendered popup; this does not select a complete feature/UI implementation or establish that every native preview feature is straightforward to reproduce. Keep the architectural alternative separate from the narrower traversal optimization. The revised discussion direction is immediate cached detail display plus equal-priority background loading/refresh, alongside investigation of opening the popup without the carousel dependency. These preferences and the user's request to propose root-cause alternatives proactively are recorded in the editable user-preference section of context.md. Manual viewing choices and actual list/profile changes retain their existing immediate handling; acceptance of cached title details does not defer those actions. The script remains 1.3.16; this is a documentation-only clarification, with no runtime tests rerun.

## Cached hover without carousel preparation — 1.4.0

**Current status: Rolled back with the 1.4.1–1.4.2 refinements in release 1.4.3 at the user's request; see the restoration record below.**

The user selected analysis followed by implementation of the revised cached-detail approach. Source analysis confirms that the native bridge depends on live source-card React handlers, identity and geometry, while serializable title data is sufficient for a script-owned information popup. No verified native title-data-only opener is available in the repository. Release **1.4.0 implements a script-owned popup for both grid sections**, removing that live-source dependency from the normal grid hover path instead of pre-traversing the carousel. The minor version reflects the new user-facing popup. Metadata/internal versions match.

- **Immediate display:** After the existing 120 ms intent dwell and scroll guards, render one popup from the already available title/artwork and any restored details. No native page lookup/movement, graft, geometry proxy or native hover replay occurs on that path. Reuse the card's current artwork URL rather than requesting larger artwork or a video. Display title, synopsis, release year, movie duration, maturity and up to four genres when available. Update only the currently owned popup as background details arrive; no full-grid refresh or regrouping is introduced. Placement is measured once per opening and clamped to the viewport. Later enrichment keeps the artwork position and limits height to the remaining space, allowing internal overflow. This measures synchronous rendering, not first paint or image decode.
- **Neutral, finite background queue:** The initial grid remains independently published. After the viewing scan finishes, request optional title metadata through the existing validated same-origin pathEvaluator transport, in original list order, fifty titles per batch with one request in flight. All initial titles have the same loading policy, including filtered series, collapsed watched titles and restored cache entries. Hovering does not reorder the queue, add requests or retry failures. A manual viewing refresh can finish before the next detail batch starts. The detail job has a thirty-second total budget and existing bounded request timeouts/caps, yields between batches, stops on a request failure, and neither writes viewing results nor changes completion criteria. A 500-title successful sweep adds ten optional requests. Optional fields may be absent/error/unsupported in the live Netflix response; basic popups and any existing cache remain usable.
- **Compact cache and scope:** Save at most 600 normalized, current-list detail records in Tampermonkey storage, keyed by Netflix profile and checked against the display locale. Retain no DOM, React objects, auth token, server payload or URL in those records. Restored records are accepted for up to thirty days and refreshed once on a later list entry, without per-hover freshness checks or periodic work. Missing fields can retain usable cached values. Revalidate profile, locale, route and current grid before publishing/saving; obsolete responses cannot update another owner. Storage failure does not disable the popup. An originally empty list starts the same path when its first title is added; a later newly added title can still show basic details until its metadata is available on a list entry.
- **Actions and ownership:** Provide movie Play and Netflix More info links. A series title id is not assumed to identify a playable episode; More info provides Netflix's own episode selection and additional native actions. Manual watched/caught-up/reset buttons delegate to the existing profile-scoped card controls, retaining grouping and scroll-preservation behavior. The popup uses a still image and does not reproduce Netflix's autoplay preview or rating/list-management controls; those remain available through Netflix's full details. Card-to-popup/controls and back retain one owner. Genuine departure, card movement/hiding, scroll/resize, source/route changes, hidden tabs and listener teardown close it. Internal popup scrolling remains usable. Keyboard card focus opens the popup; Tab reaches its actions, and Escape/Close restore focus without reopening.
- **Verification data:** performanceWork.cachedHover adds scalar open/cache/basic/update/close counts, close reasons, render elapsed totals/maxima, restored/ready/missing records, request/loading/failure state, storage/render/log failures, and usable-field counts. Separate synopsis missing/error/unsupported counts distinguish an incomplete live response from parsing expectations without copying its contents. At most 48 anonymous opening events per route report cache readiness, section and render duration with carouselRequired: false; one background completion event reports coverage, usable fields and its own network timing/concurrency. The initialization event identifies cached mode and equal priority. No per-frame positioning, polling observer, title-level log or new scroll-time grid traversal is added.

All **330 deterministic tests pass**, including eighteen new regressions for actual cached activation after dwell with throwing native-operation stubs, equal complete queues despite cache/visibility/group state, viewing-scan ordering, in-place enrichment, pointer handoffs/overflow/scroll rearming, existing manual-choice behavior, warm cache and profile/locale isolation, failed/rate-limited reads without retry or status changes, abort/stale-response cleanup, keyboard/hidden/listener lifecycle, an initially empty list's first addition, bounded reference-aware metadata/plain-text rendering, missing/error response shapes, persistence/logger failure and anonymous bounded diagnostics across all supported UI locales. The original native-hover, collection, cancellation, responsive, grouping and Undo tests remain passing. Syntax, version equality, diff review and whitespace checks apply. context.md was reviewed; the saved durable preferences remain unchanged. No assistant browser inspection or authenticated Netflix request was performed.

Next priority is the user's live check of **1.4.0 in both sections**, followed by one Copy Logs export if responsiveness, positioning or metadata coverage is incomplete. Expected evidence is cached opens increasing while normal grid hover adds zero native preparations/replays or hover carousel moves; rich coverage is assessed through synopsis/other-field counts, not merely the ready record count, which can include partial records. The removed preparation path is proven by deterministic control-flow checks, while perceived smoothness, first paint and Netflix's live optional metadata shapes remain user-owned validation. The older journey-level traversal optimization is no longer needed for the normal cached grid hover path; keep it conditional on a future selected native-popup mode rather than implementing both approaches. Original **1–8** remain implemented, **9–10** retain their image/rendering measurement gates, and the formatter idea remains unselected.

## Page scrolling through cached hover and removal of X — 1.4.1

The user reports that an open popup prevents scrolling and selects exactly two corrections: preserve page scrolling and remove the visible X because mouse-away dismissal suffices. Release **1.4.1** implements those corrections. Source review identifies the scrolling trap: the popup had an internal automatic overflow area with overscroll containment, while the document scroll handler explicitly returned for wheel/scroll targets within the popup.

The popup now clips overflow instead of providing its own user scroll area and has no overscroll containment. Wheel/scroll input follows the existing passive document listener and dismisses the cached popup before the scroll-burst shortcut, even when a popup was reopened by keyboard focus during an ongoing burst. No preventDefault, stopPropagation, synthetic wheel input, manual scrollBy call, extra listener, timer or network work is added. The existing scroll quiet/physical-intent guards continue to prevent a stationary card from reopening. The X's DOM node, click handler and styling are removed; mouse-away/blur dismissal and keyboard Escape remain, with focus restoration after Escape. Metadata loading, cache policy, viewing rules and actions are unchanged. Popup content remains bounded to its existing viewport placement; very short viewports can clip content rather than capturing page scrolling.

Copy Logs adds two scalar cachedHover counters: scrollDismissals counts owned popups dismissed by scrolling; wheelOverPopup identifies wheel input originating inside popup content. Existing closes and lastCloseReason report the same dismissal once, with no per-wheel event logging.

All **332 deterministic tests pass**. Updated regressions exercise wheel input over popup text without cancelling default or propagation, single dismissal across subsequent scroll events, no X and retained keyboard actions/Escape. Two additional regressions cover wheel and scroll over popup/outside targets during an existing burst, stationary-pointer suppression, CSS without an automatic scroll area/containment, and mouse-away dismissal in both main and watched sections. Syntax, matching increased versions, diff and whitespace checks pass. These verify code and event handling; actual browser default scrolling remains the user's live check. No browser inspection or authenticated Netflix request was performed. The durable popup interaction preference was added to the editable part of context.md; its protected prefix is unchanged.

Next priority is the user's check of **1.4.1 scrolling while pointing over the popup and mouse-away dismissal**. The broader 1.4.0 metadata/responsiveness validation remains open; no other selected or conditional performance work is added in this pass.

Source inspection confirms that cached mode creates a still-image information popup and returns before the native hover bridge: there is no subsequent native-popup transition or video-preview download. With details already restored before every opening, the absence of an observable later enrichment transition is expected. A card awaiting missing text and a card awaiting Netflix's moving preview therefore require different follow-ups; the user was asked to distinguish these observations before selecting a change.

The user's clarification resolves the distinction: they expected the cached popup to be an initial display followed by Netflix's normal popup appearance, rather than the permanently different presentation implemented in 1.4.0. Equal loading priority applies to the title metadata queue, not to loading native popup components or video previews. All records completing therefore cannot produce the expected native-looking transition. This is a mismatch in the communicated/implemented feature, not evidence of a stalled metadata queue; whether the user also wants autoplay video has not been established.

This is an assessment, not a loading fix or a claim that the reported appearance is resolved. The script remains at 1.4.1. The next design direction is a familiar Netflix-style presentation using the already loaded records; an actual native-popup takeover would require a separately verified integration and, through the existing bridge, restore its live-card/carousel dependency. Neither optional implementation is selected by this clarification. No browser inspection or authenticated Netflix request was performed. The clarified durable appearance preference is recorded in the editable part of context.md without changing its protected instructions.

## Familiar cached popup presentation — 1.4.2

## Initial title data versus native popup activation — clarification after restoration

The user questions why a two-second complete grid load does not eliminate later native carousel movement, since title information appears available already. Source review confirms that this movement is a dependency of the current hover bridge, not proof that Netflix fundamentally requires traversal or that title metadata must be downloaded again. Treat data availability, publication into Netflix's application state, and an active native hover component as separate questions.

fetchMyListCarouselPage makes a direct userscript fetch and parses the response; logical collection combines its edges. buildGraphqlMyListItems keeps only title identity/link, title text, image URL, logical position and a shared detached DOM template. It does not pass the full responses into Netflix's GraphQL rendering client/store. The persisted CarouselPage query requests artwork with fetchHighResCards disabled, but its complete live returned field coverage is not reported in existing logs; more useful data may be present than this parser retains. Do not claim that the initial response lacks every popup field. Likewise, a response reaching the browser does not establish that this code has published it to Netflix's own component state.

createItemClone copies card markup; its comment and cleanup explicitly separate that from React properties/activation state. activateClone reuses a source only when the correct native page/card is active; otherwise prepareMountedPage resolves the target live card. The bridge copies React props/fiber links, aligns that source with the grid tile and replays hover onto it. Existing identity/recycling checks explain why retaining a detached card or merely caching more fields does not make this particular path independent of the carousel. Normal preparation goes to the requested page, rather than necessarily rescanning all 500 titles. Netflix's own already-mounted cards have their native behavior available; the grid's copied artwork alone does not provide it.

The relevant alternative to investigate is opening Netflix's existing popup for a title and grid anchor using its native controller/component and available data, without needing that title's original row card mounted. No verified entry point is established by this source review. First establish bounded anonymous field coverage of the existing list responses and inspect how the native hover handler reaches its popup controller; avoid assuming that another metadata preload alone solves activation, or resuming the rejected custom renderer. Existing preparation timings measure movement/update waits, not the amount of repeated network downloading. This is a discussion/source analysis only: keep runtime 1.4.3 and existing behavior unchanged, do not add speculative code or rerun runtime tests. No browser inspection or authenticated request was performed. context.md was reviewed without a durable preference change.

### P03 — Localization, DOM names and stylesheet ownership

Transferred live resources to the five planned source files: src/dom-names.js, src/i18n/i18n.js, ui-messages.js, log-messages.js and src/grid/styles.js. Legacy imports and invokes these owners; it contains no second message table, shared DOM catalog, stylesheet or moved localization implementation. The i18n factory exposes eight display operations, keeping base-language parsing/interpolation private. The stylesheet exposes explicit idempotent installation/removal, with unchanged route timing and CSS text. Resource imports add no startup listeners, requests or observers.

The narrow locale-reader bridge calls the existing legacy getNetflixLanguage reader; P05 transfers that page interpretation to netflix/context.js. Mixed header/error assembly remains with presentation because it reads sourceState, while its count/time/number formatting now uses i18n. The old shared performance object no longer owns a competing popup counter set; collected performance summaries merge a copied inspection snapshot at the original field.

### P07 — Viewing protocol interpretation and typed reads

### P08 — Native discovery, binding, shared reads and page-model transfer

The resumed persistent goal, **Continue the migration plan**, resumes the remaining authorized migration in dependency order. Created netflix/carousel/carousel.js and page-model.js and switched live legacy callers to the sole native owner. It owns binding generations/borrowed handles, profile/index/layout interpretation, readiness/source polls, synchronous caches, discovery observer/coalesced frames and private logical maps/state. Owner checks and private callback tickets fix them. The fast-collection path requests recovery without draining queued membership work. The existing empty-versus-missing-source distinction, observer filtering/narrowing/coalescing, request/route guards and native read budgets are preserved. Model commands avoid adding DOM discovery to former direct field writes.

Six baseline scenarios transfer or are covered in carousel.test.js through the real interface: shared sample reads, indicator/profile refresh, readiness sorting/read counts, track/count/column changes, exact overlapping windows and exception/async scope cleanup. The old text/helper interception of candidate calls no longer exercises the moved owner; exhaustive page/window results and bounded input reads replace it. | withNativeReadScope, invalidateNativeReadScope, nativeRect, nativeFilledSlots, nativeIndicatorItems, currentPageSlots, visibleSignature | Existing native sampling, source resolution and interaction validation share owner reads; geometry restoration requests invalidation | P10 completes source operations; P17/P18 transfer residual interaction consumers; P20 removes composition forwarding |
| carouselMoveButton, carouselMoveButtonDisabled | Residual native navigation/traversal reads controls without owning discovery or mapping | P09 navigation and P10 collection |
| nativeCarouselReadiness, waitForNativeSource, waitForNativeCarouselReady, parseSlotLayoutFormula, measureVisibleLayout, measureEmptyLayout, readNativeMyListDomState | Initialization, mounted-fast proof, binding recovery and responsive checks consume owner results | P10 completes native readiness/resolution/remapping; P19/P20 transfer residual coordinators |
| nativeReactCarouselTotalCount, requireNativeReactCarouselTotalCount, netflixItemIndexFromSlot, logicalSlotPositions, nativeLogicalPageState, requireNativeLogicalPageState, logVirtualRawIndexDiagnostic, normalizeNetflixLogicalIndex, expectedLogicalIndicesForPage, logicalPageFromSlotPositions, wrappedTailLogicalPageInfo, wrappedTailLogicalPageForRebuild | Traversal/responsive/source-position callers consume interpreted native facts and bounded diagnostics | P10 transfers native algorithms; P17/P19 retire remaining workflow callers |
| notePage, beginCollection, markCycle, completeCollection, confirmPageCount, anchorAfterDelta, markMappingStale, deferMapping, commitMapping | Residual acknowledgement, collection, bootstrap, delta and responsive logic request controlled model changes; async callers validate borrowed owners first | P09 acknowledgement; P10 collection/source mapping |

### P10.4 — Mounted-card lookup and polling checkpoint

Continued the authorized migration goal at its first unfinished prompt checkpoint. Synchronous mounted-card lookup and the bounded mounting wait now belong to carousel. mountedCard returns the same unforgeable validated source handles used by expected-page resolution, preserving active-page filtering and exact-href/title fallback. resolveCard's explicit mounted mode owns the existing 500-ms default, caller-specific deadlines and 10-ms polling interval. It copies identity at admission and captures binding, model interpretation and page ownership; it initiates no request or navigation.

An admitted wait cannot silently adopt a replacement binding or page. Route/hover cancellation validates before another card scan, and meaningful mapping/model replacement rejects obsolete work. Each pending tick has a private idempotent ticket; bind/clearBinding closes old tickets, and closed callbacks cannot wake or release replacement waits. The scalar mountedSourceWaits diagnostic exposes current pending tickets without retaining interaction history.

### P10.5 — Preferred-page recovery and bounded native search checkpoint

Continued the migration goal at P10.5 after the verified P10.4 gate. Carousel's preferred-refresh and search resolveCard modes now own the native pulse and nearby-page recovery workflows. Preferred refresh preserves direct hits, the normal right/left adjacent pulse and the existing 700-ms mounting deadline. Search preserves preferred/right/left attempt order, radius clamping, preferred/other-page mounting deadlines of 500/280 ms and the 120-ms minimum/520-ms hydration fallback. It uses private navigation and mounted resolution rather than another queue, observer or timer owner; it adds no requests or background work.

Expected, mounted and recovery resolution share one private binding/mapping/session admission rule. Recovery retains its captured owner across queued moves, mounting, hydration and diagnostics; mounted child waits also keep their current page and validate the parent guard. Obsolete work cannot rebind between attempts, issue another queued click, read after remapping during hydration or publish after a diagnostic replaces the source. Search order is read-only. Existing diagnostic identity/page/label fields are copied scalar hints, and successful details stay trace-only; ordinary/frame lookups allocate no recovery diagnostic payload.

Native signature registration precedes creation of the returned source handles. Search returns frozen visible href/videoId/index facts and validated handles without writing membership or clone attributes. The residual locateActiveSourceItem bridge alone publishes the existing transitional page hints using itemMap lookup, validating current parent/session/source handles between updates and after logging. A parent replacement ends old publication. Lasting page hints disappear in P13, and native interaction transfers in P17.

Migration.md records these source-resolution contracts before implementation. Existing carousel.js is deepened; the target layout, 21-step order and import exceptions remain unchanged. README/docs describe actual ownership and context.md has no durable change.

| Remaining technical native surface | Public model/read commands still serve scheduled preparation/remapping callers | P10.7 caller/facade audit before P11 |

### P10.7 final transfer — Native presentation and complete parent audit

Continue selected P10.7 from clean **9b1037c**, preserving the verified **1.4.30 / 9082f52** facade checkpoint and exact Windows run **37224136139**. Carousel.presentSource owns native section/header/track markers, scan-to-parking transitions and original-source visibility. Its frozen mount lease captures route/binding generation, connected/native references and narrow caller admission; its guarded anchor supports normal headers and native empty content. A private property journal preserves original values and transfers cleanup ownership on shared elements. Replaced or interrupted owners cannot overwrite or release their replacements. Release remains available after route invalidation, attempts remaining fields after a failure and retires ownership; failures have bounded, failure-isolated counters/logging. Navigation retains its separate motion styles/FAST_MOVE_CLASS ownership. resetSource closes native presentation resources. Carousel.cleanupArtifacts owns historical source-hook/style restoration with checks between writes; historical script UI cleanup stays outside native integration.

The nativeSourcePresentation bridge retains only its releasable handle and captured parent/native guard. markOriginalHeader, beginSourceScan and parkSource now request complete owner operations. placeLegacyFrame, finalizeEmptyLegacyList, populated/empty adoption, runScript fast/native collection and applyOriginalMyListVisibility switch to the lease, rather than writing native markers directly. cleanupTargetSessionDom releases that exact lease after navigation/hover cleanup. Menu visibility works before initialization publishes a parent state and does not adopt a native binding. Status row gaps/frame attachment remain current presentation work; replacement during marker admission cannot publish status or begin collection into the replacement parent. Queued mutation identity and existing initialization retry behavior remain.

Optional observeSource presentation facts freeze hidden/parked booleans and reject changed native markers before responsive policy accepts its existing hidden-height shortcut. No raw marker read remains in that policy. CopyLogs fallback section/scroller/track discovery moves into explicitly requested passive diagnostics, preserving supplied-reference precedence. Missing or failing discovery leaves the native description unavailable without aborting other report fields. Ordinary counter-only diagnostics perform no fallback/card/geometry/marker read. Native counters additionally describe only presentation-owner count and failed restoration fields.

No direct section/header/scan/parking/track/visibility writes or animation-style cleanup remains in legacy, apart from explicitly classified script-owned synthetic/clone UI and the separate planned P17 popup alignment owner below. The generated artifact reflects the same private owner and cutover.

The new boundary initially fails for absent presentSource; passive fallback and actual CopyLogs failure isolation fail before transfer; marker observations fail before their admission; reentrant painting fails before per-paint ownership; pre-initialization visibility fails before its missing-parent guard. These regressions pass after the corresponding owner/caller changes. Added native cases exercise scan/parking/visibility baseline restoration, same-element lease replacement, native empty anchors, parent/native/route replacement during writes, restoration failure and reentrant replacement, independent navigation leases, historical cleanup cancellation, reentrant paint admission and passive fallback discovery. Actual residual cases cover visibility/status gaps and route cleanup, obsolete status publication, queued-intent preservation before collection, and preference application before parent publication. Existing CopyLogs adds fallback-failure coverage; the delayed-scroller fixture now physically mounts its declared source before the source wait returns. Responsive fixtures use actual native marker names; no existing scenario or assertion family is dropped.

This bounded checkpoint transfers one rendering/resource responsibility completely: grid/grid.js is the live capability, private cards.js owns the sole clone registry, registered immutable handles, exact record admission, startup snapshot/shared-template release, replacement/removal/insertion and retained removal trees. Cancellation, native-source replacement and construction failure preserve the old tree/view and collector material for retry. Existing shared-template construction retains only one displayed tree per title at 30/150/600 titles.

Actual native preparation now requests the one grid replacement operation. Expected card/record admission runs before capture/graft and around callbacks; stale/copy/retired handles fail. Frame invalidation prevents reentrant insertion during disposal, and native-release failures cannot prevent cleanup of the other cards or retained registry views. Removed membership records no longer store the detached tree in snapshot. Expiry releases ordinary retention. A queued admitted fallback can retain that exact correlation until insertion/intent disposal; it cannot borrow another same-ID record's tree. Clear/dispose release all remaining material. Passive active/retained card counts and retirementFailures are available through the existing CopyLogs summary. The unused createItemClone/releaseItemCardSnapshot/setGridClone bridges are removed, including their fixture-only exports; live hint publication delegates to admitted grid updates.

Migration.md records this bounded status-resource contract before extraction. Private frame.js now owns one detached/current status, its label/meta and CopyLogs nodes, status attachment/layout/typography, mismatch-dialog DOM/listeners, stylesheet acquisition and historical script-UI cleanup. All actual legacy status writes delegate to complete grid operations. Native heading typography is interpreted in page-dom as frozen copied strings, with the existing heading/fallback selection and style values preserved. The coordinator supplies captured session/parent/native observations and retains list/count formatting, mismatch decisions and responsive scheduling; it owns no status or dialog resource state.

CopyLogs delegates clipboard/report work and requested/completed/failed logging through copyDiagnosticLogs. Frame owns the click listener, latest-copy admission and the existing finite 2.5-second feedback timer. Late/out-of-order completions and obsolete timers cannot paint or relinquish a newer resource; route/disposal invalidates actions before cleanup. Starting another copy clears prior success feedback so a later failure cannot leave a permanent copied label. Dialog actions require the exact current resource and captured caller, retire both listeners after an admitted choice and preserve a newer reentrant publication. No new observer, request, polling loop or browsing scheduler is introduced.

Disposal first retires frame admission, then releases cards/native callbacks and every exact acquired resource. A failed host removal cannot prevent release of the other nodes/listeners, and passive frameReleaseFailures joins existing grid diagnostics. A newer lifecycle can explicitly acquire an existing stylesheet before old cleanup finishes; the retiring snapshot cannot remove that newly acquired resource. Cleanup never removes a replacement merely because it shares an ID. The unused ID-based removeStyles export and exact legacy -> grid/styles.js import exception are retired; styles remain private to the existing frame capability.

### P20 — Application/session composition and settings (2026-10-07)

The user selected P20 only. Starting clean revision 791bc34, release 1.4.56, passes the read-only starting `npm run check`; P19 runtime 26e1a6d and Windows run 37664915633 satisfy its dependency gate. The implement-plan skill was applied. The target layout/ownership contracts are unchanged. One implementation-order adjustment is documented in MigrationPlan.md before cutover: remove src/legacy.js with P20 rather than leaving an unreachable source file/new checker exception until P21. P21 still owns residual test support removal and the full final acceptance audit. P20 is complete, with runtime commit/push and exact Windows acceptance recorded below.

Main now starts `createApplication().start()`. Application owns startup/current route, activation revisions, exact history/popstate/hashchange resources, lazy context/i18n, retained logger, settings and the current page session. Start/dispose are idempotent; history restoration is conditional on exact wrapper ownership. Retired queued/navigation/native callbacks cannot act for a replacement page. Optional Tampermonkey grants are passed explicitly from lexical bindings, preserving their availability outside globalThis. Imports activate no requests/listeners/menus/timers.

`app/my-list-session.js` is the private composition/lifetime/transaction owner for one My List visit: initialization and deferral, count/readiness/collection strategy coordination, grid acceptance, viewing delivery, late/empty/populated/replaced-source adoption, blocked recovery, membership/native/hover material coordination, mismatch prompting/reinitialization and report/status integration. Its facade exposes start/dispose/check/preferencesChanged/copied diagnostics rather than a public sourceState or feature context bag. A new instance is assembled per visit. Each owns a separate request scope/registry; application allocates unique epoch numbers only. Old cleanup cannot abort the next page even if lifecycle callbacks reenter. Scheduled initialization callbacks check their exact timer before clearing state; cleanup retires intents/tickets before native resources and attempts independent presentation releases after host failures. Existing request/readiness/construction/recovery limits and optional viewing failure behavior are retained.

`app/settings.js` solely owns the existing preference/storage key and original-source menu, with copied semantic preferences and exact menu-revision admission. It imports/edits no feature DOM. Changes call the current session, which requests admitted carousel source visibility and grid spacing. Missing grants/denied storage preserve the default. No stale menu callback can change/persist preference after replacement/disposal.

Remaining feature algorithms/state are with their owners: list membership owns position deviation/mismatch against authoritative order; carousel owns native card identity/fallback capture and preferred-page fallback; page-DOM owns title normalization; grid owns membership order/insertion positioning; hover owns replacement hover observation; native-popup owns alignment restoration counters; list mutations owns aggregate Undo work. Page session retains cross-feature mismatch/recovery coordination and copied app recovery counters only. Responsive now receives a frozen read-only parent projection and explicit guarded layout/initial-page/viewport acceptance callbacks, with native observation/hint collaborators translated inside session composition. No responsive write authority to the internal page parent is exposed.

All live callers use the new composition. src/legacy.js and all exact legacy import exceptions are gone; dependency checks remain strict and reject a newly reintroduced legacy edge or dormant module. Runtime dependency metadata contains application/session/settings and excludes legacy. Native/data/grid/list/viewing/hover providers use their declared public boundaries without native-to-app imports, raw protocol/tree interpretation or structural card writes in orchestration. No request, observer, background work, cache/storage migration or budget increase is added. Target native popup, scrolling, grouping/filter/control/menu behavior is preserved.

All 725 existing scenarios remain retained/retargeted. Six added public scenarios cover application/session/history callback retirement, settings persistence/menu replacement/denied storage, independent page scope request ownership, grid-owned order/insertion/admission and lexical userscript grants in the generated entry. Full local release 1.4.57 passes build, read-only output/dependency check, all 731 tests (none failed/cancelled/skipped), distributable syntax and whitespace. The initial intended application boundary test failed for the missing module before implementation. Actual generated startup/route entry/exit/reentry, optional APIs/viewing behavior, delayed/replaced/blocked source recovery, pending actions and profile/old-request cancellation remain covered by retained bundle/capability/residual cases. Authored/generated/import/version/caller/state/resource/document review passes. Package/lock/internal/metadata versions agree and exceed published 1.4.56; context was reviewed with no durable preference change.

No raw protocol/React interpretation or structural card writes moved into app, and no duplicate authoritative state, scheduler, request, observer, storage migration or production inspection API was added. All 42 authored production modules are reachable with coherent use, no legacy file/import exception, external runtime dependency, forbidden edge, dormant source or cycle. ### Live evidence — 1.4.58 (2026-10-08)

The user supplied `logs/1.4.58.txt`, copied at 2026-10-08T00:00:24.153+02:00 in Firefox 157 on Windows. It is preserved unchanged and pushed under the standing logs authorization (SHA256 7D0173C467CFBBD92CCC2A8D31E2211A163EF4D5F4C0459D52578586DD872F6E). This is live evidence for the P21 release, separate from its 494 offline tests and exact Windows CI acceptance.

The script starts on /browse, enters My List through history.pushState and initializes the full 500-title grid in 3,357 ms using fresh logical GraphQL collection. The final snapshot is completed/not running, with 500 accepted/grid records, seven columns, 72 finalized logical pages and pageMappingStale=false. The displayed 167-item count is the current filtered presentation, not a partial 500-title collection. Viewing finishes 23 successful requests with peak concurrency two and no failures/rate limits/aborts: 199 completed, 270 unknown and 20 incremental publications. The 247 series diagnostic rows include 75 latest-episode-complete hints, 170 unavailable-episode-progress cases and two incomplete/inconsistent season-metadata cases. Successful transport does not establish complete viewing metadata; unknown titles remain unresolved rather than being treated as caught up.

Interaction counters show 106 queued intents, 81 cancelled intents, 25 completed dwells, 14 target clones rebuilt and 83 neighboring slots skipped. All 14 replay attempts dispatch, with zero replay failures/cancellations, 14 native exits and zero exit failures. Nine matching-preview transfers are retained and released; four replacement-pointer checks are accepted. Eleven preparations are interrupted (three scroll, six pointer leave, two controls), and their interruption logs stay within the 48-log limit. All 14 preview observations complete through nine transfers/five early releases; none performs a delayed root search. Diagnostic, alignment-restoration, grid retirement/frame/placement/material-release and reconciliation cleanup failure counters are zero; no WARN/ERROR entries appear. This supports real native hover/preview/scroll activity with the final modular release, including cancellation, rather than only startup.

Latency remains observable: main preparation reaches 1,593 ms, queued movement reaches 1,051 ms, and the slowest native move takes 1,920 ms, including 1,913 ms acknowledgement and six ms settlement. A second move takes 1,270 ms with 1,256 ms acknowledgement. Graft/alignment/replay maxima are three/seven/one ms respectively. These measurements point to native movement/acknowledgement and queue wait as substantial delay sources in this session; they do not prove smooth popup latency or a regression against a different capture. If delay remains a user-visible concern, the bounded follow-up is to examine issued-move acknowledgement/cancelled-work queue admission while preserving native settlement ownership; no runtime change is selected by the new-log prompt.

Six responsive checks are unchanged, including one ignored parked-height collapse; there are no real viewport events or refreshes. The snapshot has no pending membership intents, Undo entries or active popup/preview, and its six-millisecond construction/readiness retirement fixes are not directly tested by this live visit. Subsequent history changes remain on the target route; there is no target exit/reentry or disposal evidence. Real resize/zoom/remapping, route retirement/reentry, membership/Undo and manual viewing actions still need user-side validation. No mapping warning occurs here, but this capture does not establish that the older warning is fixed in every relevant case. Documentation/log changes only: release remains 1.4.58, authored/generated output verification passes, and context was reviewed without a durable preference change.
